APM PMS Q&A SOP: the session run on Saturday 7-Nov, on the 6-Nov close

Readings as at Fri 6-Nov-2026, Friday's close (the dashboard data built at 06:13 UK on Saturday 7-Nov). The previous note read Thu 5-Nov; this one adds one session, Friday 6-Nov, in which the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.0% (it is +0.2% over the week). Friday 6-Nov was a reading day: Industry RS below 30 and the relative 200D MA flat or falling over the month were read at this close and stand until Fri 13-Nov. The next month-end reading is Mon 30-Nov. The next Tuesday session is 10-Nov. The rules are your Position Entry and Exit Rules as changed on 2-Nov-2026.

The three things that matter most this week

1. Friday's reading sells Volex (5.49% of capital) and Sdiptech (7.43%) in full at Tue 10-Nov, because Industry RS for Industrials and capital goods read 26, below the line of 30 (46 at the 30-Oct reading, 79 on 2-Oct).

  • Why it matters: both are Middle Innings Core Investments outside the one-off in/out link, so the rule sells each whole holding at the next Tuesday session. With Arteche (3.70%) and Alfen's two tranches (3.18%) also due, the 10-Nov sales come to about 19.8% of capital, all in Industrials and capital goods.
  • The signal is the industry's, not the stocks': Sdiptech's own Stock RS is 88, a 13-month high, and its other five tests are clear; Volex's FY2027 EPS is up 15% over three months with 7 of 7 analysts raising. Volex's own trend has broken, though: its 5-Nov fall came on 3.50M shares (£18.4m), a distribution day as large as the 19-Oct update, and all three of its RS readings are at their lowest in the data file.
  • For your ruling: Volex's interims are on Thu 12-Nov, two sessions after the session the rule sells at. Sdiptech's size is in doubt: the register says 7.43% of capital, UBS put it at 6.04% of AUM on 5-Nov, and your 4-Nov note says the position is 6.0%.

2. Theon's two tranches triggered the 30% fall on Friday (close €26.74 against the €26.82 level, Stock RS 12), and Hexatronic fell 20.5% in five sessions.

  • Theon (3.46%): each tranche now sells at the Tuesday session after its release: a close at or above €30.75 (a 15% rebound) or the 63rd trading day (about 5-Feb-2027). Each tranche sells whatever the one-off in/out link says. The Q3 trading update is Monday 9-Nov after the close.
  • Hexatronic (4.49%): three heavy-volume down days closing at the low; on 6-Nov it closed below its rising absolute 200D MA (37.36) for the first time since 26-Jan. Its estimates, ratings and PTs did not move, so this is a de-rating. Relative price is 28.5% below its 52W high: at the 30-Nov month-end that gives a whole-holding sell signal unless the SP closes at 38.20 or higher that day, about 4.9% above Friday's close. I grade that month-end sell signal Probable.

3. Helios Towers' 5-Nov results broadened its case, and the SP held while the market fell on Friday; but the add is blocked, and its nearest sell test is close.

  • The case: third-quarter tenancy additions of 924 against a consensus of 662 (Jefferies, 5-Nov); FY2026 guidance tightened upward; Morgan Stanley, Jefferies and Deutsche Bank raised their PTs. The SP is +8.1% in the week and held on 6-Nov while the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.0%.
  • The limits: the holding is 7.21% of capital, above its 6.68% limit, so the rules block an add. Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read 30-Nov) has Sector RS at 35 and Stock RS at 71, two points from a sell signal; I grade this Plausible.

How this note is built

Group 1 (Market) sets the week, the fortnight and the month. Group 2 (Individual Positions) takes the 16 holdings in the order they most need your thinking: the four sales due at Tue 10-Nov first. Group 3 (Portfolio) gives the themes across holdings, each holding's add stage, the graded add and exit candidates and the proposed order of sale under the link; then what I will do unless you say otherwise, and the data faults.

This takes Alfen to zero, as your 4-Nov note asks.

Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.0% on Friday 6-Nov, which leaves it up 0.2% in the week, 2.0% over the fortnight and 3.6% over the month; over three months it is down 1.8%, over twelve months up 14.2%. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 30-Oct close, the fortnight the 10 sessions from the 23-Oct close and the month the 21 sessions from the 8-Oct close, the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's bases; the industry figures are price moves of each industry's index over the same sessions (the Market chart, 3 and 12 months).

  • The month's leaders (21 sessions to 6-Nov): Healthcare +10.8%, Consumer staples +8.7%, Consumer discretionary +7.9%, Real assets +7.1%, Professional, business and consumer services +6.7% and Infrastructure +6.5%.
  • The month's laggards: Technology (4.1%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (3.3%), Transportation (2.1%) and Industrials and capital goods (0.4%).
  • The week: Media +4.6%, Consumer discretionary +3.2%, Real assets +3.1% and Consumer staples +2.3% led; Defence (5.4%), Technology (4.1%), Energy, commodities and metals mining (3.4%), Transportation (3.2%) and Industrials and capital goods (0.8%) fell.
  • Friday 6-Nov: every industry but Utilities (+0.1%) fell; Professional, business and consumer services (1.7%), Transportation (1.6%), Media (1.6%) and Defence (1.3%) led the fall; Healthcare (0.2%) and Real assets (0.3%) fell least.
  • Over three months: Healthcare +8.0%, Consumer staples +6.0% and Transportation +5.1% lead; Defence (13.2%), Real assets (5.3%), Technology (3.8%), Materials and Industrials and capital goods (2.8%) lag.

The defensive rotation held for a second week: Healthcare (+10.8%) and Consumer staples (+8.7%) lead the month, and on Friday's broad fall Healthcare fell least. Carlsberg (+5.7% in the week) and CVS Group (+3.4%) moved with it; Hilton Food (+2.2%) and Fevertree (+0.9%) lagged Consumer staples' +2.3%, and Xvivo fell 3.7% while Healthcare rose 1.8%. What would confirm the rotation: Consumer staples and Healthcare keeping their month's gains through the 13-Nov Friday reading; a heavy-volume rally led by Technology would say it is exhausted.

The AI and data-centre complex kept falling, and it carries three holdings: Volex and Arteche in Industrials and capital goods, Hexatronic in Technology. Technology fell 4.1% in the week and is the worst industry over the month; Volex fell 7.5% in the week, Arteche 11.4% and Hexatronic 20.5%. On Friday the large grid and cable names bounced (Siemens Energy +3.7%, Nexans +4.4%) while Arteche and Hexatronic fell, so this week's falls are partly the stocks' own; the sources read name no company news for any of the three. Morgan Stanley raised Legrand's EPS estimates and cut its PT "on sector de-rating" (Morgan Stanley, 6-Nov, via your weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026): estimates up and multiples down at once, the pattern in Volex and Hexatronic. What would confirm a sector-wide de-rating: a third week of Technology falling on heavy volume; a heavy-volume up day in Prysmian and Corning would point the other way.

Industrials and capital goods, the book's largest industry (about 23.5% of capital on current values: Sdiptech 7.43%, Volex 5.49%, IMCD 3.74%, Arteche 3.70%, Alfen 3.18%), read Industry RS 26 at Friday's reading, from 46 at 30-Oct and 79 on 2-Oct. The industry's index fell only 0.8% in the week and 0.4% over the month: its Industry RS fell because other industries rose, not because it collapsed. The reading sells Volex and Sdiptech in full at Tue 10-Nov.

Defence fell 5.4% in the week and 13.2% over three months, and its Industry RS is 0. Theon fell 12.1% in the week, 2.2 times Defence; Hensoldt and Exosens fell almost as far. The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting table holds Defence at Underweight.

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of the ideal portfolio (the table set on 6-Nov-2026): Overweight Consumer staples and Energy, commodities and metals mining; Double Underweight Consumer discretionary and Real assets; Underweight Defence, Media and, new this week, Telecoms (its relative strength uptrend broke on every pre-set measure with 2027 estimates intact); Neutral elsewhere, Industrials and capital goods included; Momentum Underweight and Fragility Double Underweight. Its logic, in brief: risk-off beneath an index near its high (the Stoxx Europe 600 0.9% from its 52W high with breadth at or below the tenth percentile of near-high days, defensives leading on all five windows): own the defensives with rising estimates, avoid the leveraged and rate-sensitive. This week's tape again rewarded the table only in part: Overweight Consumer staples +2.3% and Underweight Defence (5.4%) against +0.2% for the index, but the Underweight Media (+4.6%) and Double Underweight Consumer discretionary (+3.2%) and Real assets (+3.1%) led, and Overweight Energy, commodities and metals mining fell 3.4%. After Tuesday's sales the book's Consumer staples weight (Carlsberg, Hilton Food, Fevertree, about 11.9%) would be about three times its Industrials and capital goods weight (IMCD, 3.74%).

The index is +14.2% over twelve months and +3.6% over the month, but (1.8%) over three months, and the Portfolio Fitness for Fighting table puts it 0.9% from its 52W high on narrow breadth: a high made by fewer stocks.

For the industry's index the data say a pause ((0.4%) over the month), but for the holdings in it the trend has broken: Volex, Arteche and Hexatronic made lower lows on heavy volume this week.

Volex

Middle Innings Core Investment · 5.49% of capital in 3 tranches (tranche 1, 2.93%, bought 10-Jul-2026; tranche 2, 1.58%, bought 5-Aug-2026; tranche 3, 0.99%, bought 29-Oct-2026) · readings as at Fri 6-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

Volex's retest of its rising absolute 200D MA has failed and the break now has volume behind it: Volex closed at 520p on 6-Nov, its lowest close since 3-Aug-2026, 6.7% below that MA, after a 5-Nov fall on 3.50M shares, 3.83 times its 50D average.

  • Volex is 25.6% below its 699p closing high of 6-Oct: (7.5%) over the week, (23.5%) over the month, (6.6%) over 3 months, +42.3% over 12 months; since 28-May it has swung between 497p and 699p.
  • Volex's legs since 6-Oct: 699p to 532p (22-Oct), (23.9%); 532p to 590p (29-Oct), +10.9%; 590p to 520p (6-Nov), (11.9%), five down days in six.
  • Volex's 7.5% fall this week, once the part explained by the market is removed (beta 1.49 to the Stoxx Europe 600), is 1.3 times its usual weekly move, below your 27-Oct guide for further research; the 20-session move (2.2 times its usual size) was researched in the previous run.

Leg by leg, the latest leg is the basket's with one Volex-specific day.

  • 30-Oct (4.7%): Industrials and capital goods (1.3%), Stoxx Europe 600 (0.4%), cohort peers (2.1%).
  • 4-Nov (3.0%): the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8% on defensives; Industrials (0.3%); cohort peers (3.3%), DiscoverIE (5.8%).

  • Hypotheses, ranked: (1) a rotation out of AI data-centre and technology hardware into defensives: the cohort peers fell with Volex on 30-Oct, 4-Nov and 5-Nov, and Technology on 30-Oct (2.1%) and 5-Nov (2.5%); against it, Technology rose 0.3% on 4-Nov and the peers rose on 6-Nov while Volex fell. (2) Holders cutting ahead of the 12-Nov results: Volex fell 4.5 points more than its peers since 29-Oct and missed their 6-Nov bounce. No Volex news is in the sources read. Test: Volex's 12-Nov move against the basket's.

Developments (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026 was read): "Volex PLC — No Material Weekly Developments". No Volex news flow, sell-side note or filing in the week to about 6-Nov. Standing: 19-Oct, the trading update (Sector Digest insight, 3-Nov), profit guided "towards the upper end" but data-centre revenue down on the prior half and about $10m pulled forward (Volex (11.6%)); 30-Oct, H1 FY2027 interim results set for Thursday 12-Nov-2026 (IRPRESS, in Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026). The cause of the 5 and 6-Nov moves is not in the sources read; a web search for Volex news dated 5-Nov found nothing.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "[-] Volex bouncing between the rising 200 and 150D after a large 20% fall through Oct-26. Must get comfortable with SS momentum etc." The bounce has ended: Volex is 6.7% below the rising absolute 200D MA (557.27p) and 11.9% below the rising 150D MA (589.91p); the retest you asked the APM to confirm on 28-Oct did not hold. The sell-side half: unchanged and unmoved by the fall (items 4 to 6).

The MAs: Volex is below every MA, and the short MAs crossed the long ones on 6-Nov. On 6-Nov the 20D MA (581.5p) crossed below the rising 150D (589.91p) and the 10D (556.5p) below the absolute 200D. The 50D (612.34p) and 100D (578.21p) are falling; the 150D and absolute 200D still rise, and the absolute 200D keeps rising through mid-November because the closes it drops are 438p to 465p. Precedent: every low of the advance since mid-2025 held above the rising absolute 200D MA (17-Mar 9.3% above, 8-Jul 4.1%, 24-Aug 5.9%); the closes below it on 22, 23, 26-Oct and 4 to 6-Nov are Volex's first since 5-Jun-2025.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Volex against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; +2.8% over 20 sessions, slowing. The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes. The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: passes, but the 50D is falling.
  • The SP above each MA: fails; 6.7% below the absolute 200D MA, 10.1% below the 100D, 11.9% below the 150D, 15.1% below the 50D.
  • Near its 52W high: fails; 25.6% below 699p. Away from its 52W low: passes; 40.9% above 368.96p (10-Nov-2025).
  • Stage 3, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, especially after good news: present; 19-Oct, (11.6%), on profit guided "towards the upper end".
  • Stage 3, wider swings: present; (25.6%) against 6% to 7% pullbacks in August and September. Lower lows: present; 524p and 520p below 22-Oct's 532p. More down days than up days: present; 14 against 9 since 6-Oct.
  • Stage 3, a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: absent; the 6-Oct high was an accumulation day. The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: present; Volex sat on the absolute 200D MA from 30-Oct to 3-Nov, then broke it.
  • Verdict: a broken trend on the medium-term tests, now confirmed by volume; the long-term structure (rising 150D and absolute 200D MAs) is not yet broken. Four of six trend-health tests pass, two fail; five of six Stage 3 flags show. The lower low is no longer light-volume (item 2 (Volume) gives the 5-Nov correction). What would settle it: a close below 497p (the 8-Jul low) breaks the July base too; a heavy-volume up day back above 557p, most plausibly on 12-Nov, would reopen a retest.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: Volex's volume ratios leave out the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep, which lift the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's 50D average; the dashboard's figure follows in brackets.

A data correction changes last run's reading: 5-Nov was not a light-volume day but Volex's heaviest selling day since the 19-Oct trading update. The previous run's chart file held about 0.83M shares for 5-Nov; today's holds 3.50M (£18.35m), 3.83x (3.35x) the 50D average, so the previous note's "lower low on below-average volume" no longer stands. DiscoverIE's and Halma's 5-Nov volumes rose the same way: most likely a partial day for London listings in the earlier file.

Volex's heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more): seven, five of them down or flat, and the two largest bracket the decline.

  • Up: 6-Oct, the 699p high, +3.2%, 1.36M shares (£9.5m), 1.64x (1.41x), close at 0.90 of the range; 16-Oct, +0.5%, 1.39M (£8.9m), 1.73x (1.48x), close at 0.41.
  • Down or flat: 8-Oct (1.0%), 1.80M (£12.2m), 2.23x (1.91x); 15-Oct (5.9%), 1.85M (£11.7m), 2.34x (2.00x), a 1.3% gap down in a US-led technology sell-off (Hargreaves Lansdown, 15-Oct, read in the previous run); 19-Oct (11.6%), 3.28M (£18.4m), 3.80x (3.29x), closing at the day's low on the trading update; 20-Oct (0.2%), 2.12M (£11.9m), 2.37x (2.07x).
  • 5-Nov: (4.6%), 3.50M (£18.4m), 3.83x (3.35x), a 1.1% gap down, an intraday low of 515p, close at 0.20 of the range; no Volex news in the sources read.
  • 6-Nov, the day after: (0.8%), 0.92M shares (£4.8m), 1.01x (0.88x), range 515p to 528p, close at 0.38: the 515p low held, but no buyer lifted the close.

Distribution days in 25 sessions (dashboard): 5 (8, 15, 19 and 20-Oct, and 5-Nov), up from 4 at the 30-Oct reading and 2 at 9-Oct. They cluster around Volex's update (15 to 20-Oct) and a basket sell-off (5-Nov); 8-Oct leaves the window on 12-Nov, 15-Oct on 19-Nov.

Up-day against down-day volume is weaker than in any Volex decline of the last year.

  • Over 20 sessions the up-day to down-day volume ratio is 0.65 on both bases. Over 5 sessions it is 0.45 on the chart file, average up-day volume against average down-day volume, the basis of the 0.69, 0.76 and 0.99 below (one up day of 0.68M shares against four down days averaging 1.50M; on total volumes it is 0.11); the dashboard's 0.0 does not match those volumes (a data point).
  • The whole decline from 6-Oct: 0.69 without mechanical-volume days (14 down days, 5 heavy; 9 up, 1 heavy), against 0.76 for the May to July decline and 0.99 for February to March; Volex's advances since September 2025 ran at 1.13 to 1.51.
  • The second leg down (29-Oct to 6-Nov) averages 1.34M shares on its five down days, the same as the average on the first leg's nine down days (6 to 22-Oct): the drift of 30-Oct to 4-Nov turned into renewed institutional selling on 5-Nov.

Volume at the MAs: every break came on more volume than the reclaim before it. The 50D, 100D and 150D MAs broke on 19-Oct on 3.80x (3.29x); the 100D and 150D reclaim on 29-Oct (tranche 3's purchase day) came on 0.50x (0.43x); the absolute 200D MA was reclaimed on 27-Oct on 0.86x (0.76x), lost again on 4-Nov on 0.90x (0.78x), and the follow-through on 5-Nov came on 3.83x (3.35x).

Volume has not dried up through the move: Volex's 20D average volume is 1.30 times its 50D average on 6-Nov without the mechanical-volume days (1.14 times with them; the dashboard's trend reading, 1.01, is on an unstated basis); a healthy pullback dries up into the low.

Precedent: Volex's last two declines ended with heavy-volume up days within six sessions of the low; none has shown in the eleven sessions since 22-Oct. After the 8-Jul low (497p): 2.0x, 2.3x and 1.7x up days; after the 17-Mar low (426p): 1.7x on the fourth session and 3.7x (+8.1%) on the sixth. Since 22-Oct the best up day reached 1.28x (1.12x). A decline as deep as May to July's (28.6%) would end at about 499p, beside the 8-Jul low.

Relative price confirmed the SP's lower low, and it never confirmed the 6-Oct high. Volex's relative price is 28.0% below its 28-May 52W high (21.6% at the 30-Oct reading) and 11.5% below its relative 200D MA, lost on 30-Oct; both relative price and SP are at their lowest since 3-Aug-2026. At the 6-Oct SP high relative price stood 1.1% short of its own high.

Peers' volume: on 5-Nov DiscoverIE (2.0%) on 3.0x and Halma (3.6%) on 2.9x sold heavily with Volex: a London-listed basket seller is more likely than one aimed at Volex alone.

What volume would confirm: a turn: an up day on about 1.3M shares or more (1.5x without the mechanical-volume days) closing in the top third of the range, most plausibly on the 12-Nov results. The move continues: a close below 515p, then 497p, on above-average volume, or a sixth distribution day.

3. RS

All three of Volex's RS readings are at their lowest in the data file (from October 2025) and below every line the Sell Criteria read: Stock RS 46, Sector RS 45, Industry RS 26 on Friday 6-Nov.

  • Volex's Stock RS: 97 on 9-Oct, 65 on 23-Oct, 85 on 29-Oct, 78 at the 30-Oct reading, 60 on 4-Nov, 46 on 6-Nov (66 on 7-Aug): 51 points in four weeks, below both the 70 and 50 lines.
  • Volex's Sector RS (Engineered components, polymers and electronics manufacturing): 74 on 9-Oct, 70 at 30-Oct, 56 on 4-Nov, 45 on 6-Nov (79 on 7-Aug), below the 50 line.
  • Volex's Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 79 on 9-Oct, 46 at 30-Oct, 26 on 4 to 6-Nov (53 on 7-Aug); Friday's 26 is the sell signal. Industry RS moves in steps of about 6.7 points, one place among 16 industries.

The order of the turn: the industry first, then Volex, then its sector: a rotation carrying the stock. Industry RS fell first (79 to 66 by 16-Oct, while Volex's Stock RS was still 94); Volex's Stock RS fell 29 points in its update week (65 on 23-Oct); the sector held 69 to 79 until the 30-Oct reading (70) and has fallen 25 points since. Over the last 20 sessions the Market chart shows Healthcare +8.9% and Consumer staples +8.2% against the Stoxx Europe 600's +2.7%, Industrials and capital goods' (2.0%) and Technology's (5.1%); in the week to 6-Nov Technology fell 4.1% and Industrials 0.8% while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 0.2%.

Volex's peers' Stock RS fell with it, which supports the rotation reading: Huber + Suhner's fell from 60 to 7, DiscoverIE's from 93 to 69 and AQ Group's from 81 to 55 in a month; TT Electronics, at 92, and Solid State, at 42 (up from 25), are the exceptions.

Precedent: when Volex's Industry RS last stood near the line, at 40 (13 to 31-Mar-2026), Volex's Stock RS held 84 to 86 and Volex rose 63% from its 17-Mar low: the stock then led against its industry. This time the stock and the sector have broken with the industry, so the precedent does not help Volex. Test: Volex's Stock RS on and after the 12-Nov results, against its peers'.

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus did not move this week while the SP fell 7.5%; the estimates still rise at every horizon over 1, 3 and 6 months, at a one-month pace slowed to 2% to 3%.

  • Volex's FY2027 (year to March 2027) EPS, $0.521: +2.9% over 1 month, +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6 months. FY2028 EPS, $0.579: +2.5%, +14.4%, +21.1%. FY2029 EPS, $0.638: +2.2%, +13.1% over 3 months (first estimate 30-Apr).
  • Volex's FY2027 revenue, $1,411m: +1.8%, +6.8%, +12.2% over the same three periods; FY2027 EBITDA, $192.6m: +2.0%, +10.5%, +21.4%; FY2028 and FY2029 at about the same pace (7 analysts each).
  • Trajectory: the later years rise a little more slowly than FY2027 over 3 months (EPS +15.0%, +14.4% and +13.1%), so implied FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth eased from 11.7% (31-Jul) to 11.1%, and FY2028 to FY2029 from 11.6% to 10.3%: analysts are raising the level faster than the trajectory, a mild pulling forward of earnings.
  • Margin: Volex's implied FY2027 EBITDA margin is 13.7% (13.2% at 31-Jul), FY2028's 13.8% and FY2029's 13.9%: the upgrades are margin first, EBITDA rising faster than revenue.
  • A data point: the data file's daily estimate series are not joined to its month-end series, so a flat week may be the feed rather than the analysts.

The SP led the estimates up to 6-Oct and has fallen 25.6% since while FY2027 EPS rose about 3%: either the SP is leading the estimates down, or the market has de-rated estimates it still believes. On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Almost always, price moves first" and "price leads estimates". Hypothesis 1, price leads: the market is discounting a FY2028 risk consensus does not show, the largest customer (about 18.7% of FY2026 revenue, in effect the data-centre line) at renewal in the 2026 to 2028 re-award window (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov). Hypothesis 2, de-rating: the chain is upgraded and de-rated at once (item 9 (Peers)). Against hypothesis 1: FY2027 profit guided "towards the upper end" on 19-Oct, 7 of 7 raising. The tests are the memo's, all dated 12-Nov: H1 underlying operating profit at or above $74m; working capital falling from 28.4% of revenue; the data-centre share of Complex Industrial Technology restated.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Volex passes it (FY2027 EPS +15.0% over 3 months, +20.0% over 6); the memo expects the 3-month figure to fall to low single digits by late January on window arithmetic alone.

Relative price confirmed the 14-Oct high: Sdiptech's relative price made its 52W high that day, is 5.6% below it on 6-Nov (4.8% on 5-Nov, 6.6% at the 30-Oct month-end) and 7.5% above its relative 200D MA, which rose 2.76% over the month. No cohort peer traded heavily with Sdiptech on 14, 23 or 27-Oct, except Storskogen on its own report day (1.7x on 23-Oct).

Confirms the advance resumes: a close above 270.6 on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more (about 123,000 shares, SEK 33m). Confirms a top: a close below the 50D MA (254.64) and the 22-Oct low (252.4) on 1.5x or more.

3. RS

Sdiptech's Stock RS and its Industry RS are at the widest gap in the data file's 13 months, and the rule reads the industry: Stock RS 88, its 13-month high, against Industry RS 26, its 13-month low.

  • Stock RS: 88 on 6-Nov, from 84 at the 30-Oct month-end, 81 four Fridays ago (9-Oct), 70 three months ago (31-Jul) and 12 a year ago (31-Oct-2025); 70 or more at every reading since 25-Sep; 18 points above the line of 70 and 38 above 50
  • Sector RS (Diversified industrial groups): 68 on 6-Nov, from 80 on 4-Nov, 72 at the month-end and 89 on 16-Oct; 18 points above the line of 50, and it fell 12 points in two sessions
  • Industry RS (Industrials and capital goods): 26 at the Friday 6-Nov reading, from 46 at the 30-Oct reading, 79 on 9-Oct and 53 on 7-Aug; the previous 13-month low was 40 (March 2026). Industry RS is a rank among 16 industries in steps of about 6.7: 26 is 12th place, and 11th (about 33) would have cleared the line

The order of the turn: the industry first (after 9-Oct), the sector second (after 16-Oct), Sdiptech not at all. Industry RS read 79 (9-Oct), 66 (16-Oct), 46 (23 and 30-Oct), then 26 from 4-Nov, a day industrials fell 0.3% and the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 1.8%. Over the month industrials fell 0.4%, Sdiptech rose 4.7%. In its cohort only Sdiptech's Stock RS is higher than a month ago (Indus Holding 89 from 97, Storskogen 67 from 74). The APM's Industry Digest note of 6-Nov-2026 reads the RS table as "refusing capex-cycle industrials: trucks, rail and heavy vehicles, mining equipment and electrical equipment", while the market pays for healthcare, consumer leisure and freight. On 3-Nov you wrote: "Otherwise, Industrials are weak sectorially". Sdiptech's infrastructure niches are not in that capex-cycle list.

Hypotheses, ranked: (1) an industry rotation that has not touched a stock-specific story: Stock RS rose 7 points while Industry RS fell 53 over four weeks, and FY2027 EPS rose 7.9% in 3 months; against it, Sector RS fell 12 points in two sessions as the large diversified groups fell (item 9 (Peers)), so the selling is nearing Sdiptech's own group. (2) A lag: Sdiptech's beta of 1.29 is measured with the previous day's market move included (1.02 if only the same day's market move is used), so the stock may follow the market a day late; against it, four weeks of industry weakness have not reached it.

Precedent: at the March 2026 low in Industry RS (40), Sdiptech's Stock RS rose from 38 to 56 inside its 45.6% advance. Industry RS has not been below 30 in the data file's 13 months, so the rule has never fired on this holding.

4. Consensus by named FY

Nothing changed on Friday: a weaker FY2026 EPS and a raised trajectory for FY2027 and FY2028. The daily series is flat on 6-Nov; the key-days table dates FY2026 EPS's (1.1%) step to 6-Nov, but the daily series has it on 5-Nov (13.31 to 13.16).

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS (8.0%) in a month to SEK 13.16 (from 14.30 at 30-Sep), reversing an 8.1% rise in September, so (0.5%) over 3 months and (15.5%) over 12 months; revenue +2.9% to SEK 5,210m; EBITDA +3.1% to SEK 1,214m. EBITDA rose, so the cut to EPS arises below the EBITDA line
  • FY2027: EPS +2.1% in a month to SEK 17.79 (+7.9% over 3 months, +8.5% over 6); revenue +5.2% to SEK 5,647m (+6.8% over 3 months); EBITDA +2.8% to SEK 1,416m (+4.0%)
  • FY2028: EPS +3.7% in a month to SEK 19.97 (+9.2% over 3 months); revenue +5.9% to SEK 6,047m; EBITDA +3.4% to SEK 1,523m

The later year rises faster, so analysts are raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 rose from 10.6% (30-Sep) to 12.3% (6-Nov); implied revenue growth from FY2026 to FY2027 from 6.1% to 8.4%.

The margin keeps slipping. Sdiptech's implied FY2027 EBITDA margin (EBITDA over revenue) is 25.1%, from 25.7% at 30-Sep and 25.9% at 30-Apr; FY2028's is 25.2%, from 25.8%. The third-quarter report explains it: Supply Chain & Transportation's margin fell to 18.5% from 21.0% (cost increases at Mecno), and organic adjusted EBITA grew 6% against organic sales growth of 13% (earnings review).

The FY2026 EPS cut has named parts worth about 3%, not 8%: the SEK 13m bond-redemption charge cut 2026 adjusted EPS by about 2%, and DNB Carnegie trimmed its 2026 adjusted EPS 1% on higher segment costs (earnings review). Hypothesis: the rest comes from differences in how brokers define EPS; the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (31-Oct) calls FactSet's EPS consensus "a median of two brokers on two different EPS definitions". Test: FY2027 EPS rising while FY2026 settles.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Sdiptech passes it: FY2027 EPS up 7.9% over 3 months and 8.5% over 6, revenue 6.8% and 10.7%.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Net Upgrade Breadth is broad on revenue and EBITDA and narrow on EPS and PT, the order of item 4 (Consensus by named FY): upgrades that stop short of earnings and value. All these readings are unchanged on 6-Nov.

  • Revenue (FY2027): +83% (5 raising, none cutting, of 6), from +67% at both the September and October month-ends (the 100-day count that the test of SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50 reads stood at +71% at 30-Oct)
  • EBITDA (FY2027): +83% (5 of 6 raising), from +67% in September
  • EPS (FY2027): +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5), from +80% in September and +100% in April and May
  • PT: +43% (3 raising, none cutting, of 7), from +100% in May and June
  • Buys: 5 of 6; the first non-Buy in 24 months appeared at the October month-end

Where analysts are cutting, and why. One analyst cut FY2027 EPS; the sources read name neither that analyst nor the one behind the non-Buy. DNB Carnegie (Carl Korsheden) trimmed 2026 adjusted EPS 1% on higher segment costs and his PT to SEK 340 from 342, "citing a reduction in peer group multiples" (earnings review, 23-Oct). Morgan Stanley did the same for Legrand: it raised its EPS for 2026 to 2028 by 2.0% to 2.6% and cut its PT to €160 "on sector de-rating" (Morgan Stanley, 6-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). Hypothesis: brokers are cutting industrial multiples while raising estimates, the price side of the rotation that sent Industry RS to 26. Against it, RedEye raised its Sdiptech base case to SEK 315 (29-Oct).

The average PT fell 1.5% when a seventh analyst arrived, not on a cut. It moved from 312.7 (26 to 28-Oct, 6 analysts) to 308.1 (2-Nov, 7 analysts) with no PT cut counted. If the other six were unchanged, the seventh analyst's PT is about SEK 281 (7 times 308.1 less 6 times 312.7), 7.6% above Friday's close: the most cautious view on Sdiptech, and possibly the source of the non-Buy.

Ratings carry no signal; the PT does the work. Every analyst rated Sdiptech a Buy through its 35% fall to 167.0 (9-Feb-2026) and its rise since; the average PT followed the SP, down 35% from 398.8 (August 2024) to 258.4 (January 2026), up 19% since.

Hypothesis: the average PT has capped Sdiptech's SP when it stood about 12% to 16% above it, and 5-Nov fits. Three times in 2026 Sdiptech came that close (25-May, PT 12.1% above the close; the August month-end, 15.8%; 14-Oct, 12.7%); each time the SP then fell 6.6% to 7.8%, and analysts then raised the PT. On 5-Nov the PT stood 14.5% above the 269.2 intraday high, and the SP turned down from it; Friday's gap between the average PT and the close is 18.2%. Test: a close above 270.6 with no PT rise breaks the pattern.

Precedent: Sdiptech's SP has turned with breadth (January to April 2026: EPS breadth from (100%) to +100%, PT from (40%) to +83%, SP +45.6%). Test: EPS and PT Net Upgrade Breadth at the 30-Nov month-end.

What the rules say for Sdiptech now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • The rules sell the whole Sdiptech holding at the Tuesday 10-Nov session, on its industry alone. Industry RS for Industrials and capital goods read 26 at the Friday 6-Nov reading, below 30. Sdiptech is not under the one-off in/out link, so no replacement comes first. Its other five tests are clear, and its own Stock RS (88) is at its 13-month high
  • Sdiptech's own readings argue the other way, with negatives in the detail. All six trend-health tests pass and FY2027 and FY2028 EPS rose 2.1% and 3.7% in a month; but the SP turned down from 269.2 on light volume, four Stage 3 (topping) signs are partly present, and EPS and PT Net Upgrade Breadth have halved since the spring
  • The size the rule sells is in doubt: 7.43% of capital in the register, about 6.0% on UBS and your 4-Nov note (For your ruling, below)

Each tranche today: all five sell at the Tuesday 10-Nov session under the rules (tranche 1, 3.19%; tranche 2, 1.13%; tranche 3, 1.08%; tranche 4, 1.03%; tranche 5, 1.00%), including tranche 5, bought on 4-Nov at about SEK 266, 2.1% above Friday's close of 260.6.

What the rules say about the signal: a Friday reading sells at the following Tuesday session; no step in the rules lets a later reading (Industry RS at 30 or more on Fri 13-Nov) cancel it. You may overrule a signal; an overrule is recorded with its reason and tracked against what the rule would have done. No Sdiptech event falls before the session (fourth-quarter report 10-Feb-2027, confirmed).

The other sell tests, nearest first:

  • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70, 30-Nov: Sector RS 68 (72 a week ago, 86 four weeks ago, 56 thirteen weeks ago) would have to fall to below 50 and Stock RS 88 (84, 81, 75, at the same dates) to below 70, 19 points or more each (to 49 and 69)
  • Relative price more than 25% below its 52W high, 30-Nov: 5.6% below (6.6% a week ago, 1.9% four weeks ago, 9.1% thirteen weeks ago); if the market is flat, a close near 207.1, 20.5% below Friday's close of 260.6
  • How they interact: industrial weakness moves Industry RS and Sector RS together, but Stock RS has ignored both for four weeks; every Sdiptech-specific test needs a fall of over 20%
  • The record: none; Industry RS has not been below 30 in the data file's 13 months

An interaction: Sdiptech stays in the Pool of Stocks Eligible for Middle Innings Portfolio Selection (Industry RS is not a Pool test), so the entry rules would allow a buy-back that the exit rule would sell again at the next Friday reading below 30.

For your ruling, the size: the register records tranches 4 (30-Oct) and 5 (4-Nov) at 1.00% each at entry, 7.43% of capital now. On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I added 60bps at ~266 SEK at market to take position to 6.0% of AUM"; on 31-Oct, "Currently 5% of AUM". UBS put Sdiptech at 6.04% of AUM on 5-Nov against the register's 7.51%, a gap of about one tranche plus 0.4 points on tranche 5. If UBS is right, the sale is about 6.0% of AUM.

The next reading day and the add: Friday 13-Nov reads Industry RS and the relative 200D MA (rising 2.76% over the month). 2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: ratios leave out the two mechanical-volume days in Arteche's 50D average (31-Aug and the 18-Sep index review: about 24,800 shares a day without them, 31,900 with), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure in brackets. 18-Sep leaves the 50D window on 27-Nov, the last session before the 30-Nov month-end.

Selling volume in Arteche has doubled since October while buying has not come: the week to 6-Nov averaged 29,200 shares a day, against 13,500 in the week to 30-Oct, and the one up day traded below average. That is the opposite of a decline drying up.

Heavy-volume days (1.5x or more) in the last 25 sessions: one, 5-Nov (40,032 shares, €1.11m, 1.61x (1.26x); a 4.5% fall that opened 1.4% higher, traded up to €29.8 and closed at 0.25 of the day's range). 6-Nov: Arteche fell 2.2% on 29,416 shares (€0.80m), 1.17x (0.92x). It opened 0.4% higher at €27.8, the day's high, fell to €26.7 and closed at 0.36 of the range: a second gap up in two days sold from the open. The €26.7 intraday low was 1.2% above the €26.39 level of the test of a tranche 30% below its highest close since purchase.

Distribution days on your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average or more, in the last 25 sessions): two, 2-Nov (1.41x) and 5-Nov (1.61x), both in the last five sessions; 6-Nov (1.17x) just missed. A month ago (9-Oct) there was one (1-Oct), which has since left the window. No accumulation day has come in the 25 sessions on either basis. The dashboard counts no distribution day in 25 sessions, although 5-Nov qualifies on its own basis (1.26x): a data fault, still open.

Up-day volume as a share of down-day volume (average shares on up days over average shares on down days): 0.72 over 5 sessions (the dashboard's figure), 0.83 over 20, and 0.77 over the whole decline from 30-Sep (10 up days averaging 18,700 shares, 17 down days 24,200). In the May to July 2026 decline (8-May to 29-Jul, without the 25-Jun placement) the ratio was 0.99: buyers are thinner in this decline than in the last one of similar size.

Volume at the MAs: the breaks of the 50D and absolute 200D MAs (22 and 30-Oct) came on light volume, 0.79x and 0.60x; the moves away from them came on 1.41x (2-Nov), 1.61x (5-Nov) and 1.17x (6-Nov).

On 2-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I read the volume charts L3M positively - why does this clash with the SP trends + the APM comments". Your reading was right for October's selling and is no longer right: from 12 to 30-Oct Arteche's selling was light, and the 18-Sep index day in the dashboard's 50D average made it look about 20% lighter still; since 2-Nov the selling has turned heavy, and no up day in the whole decline has reached 1.25x.

Precedent: how Arteche's 2026 declines ended. The June to July decline ended with a 6.1% up day on 2.09x on 30-Jul, the session after the low, closing at its high; the May to June decline ended quietly and its light-volume rebound failed. The 5 and 6-Nov sessions are neither: a heavy down day through both summer lows, then a moderate-volume down day below them, each a gap up sold, with no reversal yet.

Arteche's relative price is 42.6% below its 52W high (12-May-2026), at its lowest since 9-Mar-2026, and 17.0% below its relative 200D MA, which it crossed below on 22-Oct. It was 34.7% below its 52W high at the 30-Oct month-end reading and 18.8% below at 30-Sep. Relative price confirmed the SP's new low, as it confirmed the SP's 8-May high.

Peers' volume makes 5-Nov a sector selling day and 6-Nov a sector buying day that Arteche missed. On 6-Nov Nexans rose 4.4% on 2.0x, Siemens Energy 3.7% on 1.7x and NKT 2.1% on 1.5x (simple 50D averages, chart files), while Arteche fell.

4. Consensus by named FY

Arteche's consensus has not moved for five and a half weeks: the SP has fallen 28.1% from its 30-Sep high on flat estimates. FY2026 EPS (€1.077), FY2027 EPS (€1.333) and FY2028 EPS (€1.56) are unchanged since 30-Sep, with three analysts on each; revenue and EBITDA for all three FYs are also unchanged over one month.

  • Over 3 months the nearer years' EPS rose fastest: FY2026 +9.3%, FY2027 +5.4%, FY2028 +1.3%. Over 6 months FY2026 and FY2027 EPS rose 17.5% and 17.6% while FY2028 EPS fell 1.3%: analysts pulled earnings forward rather than raising the trajectory.
  • FY2028 EBITDA rose 10.8% over 3 months against FY2028 EPS +1.3%, but the comparison spans a change in coverage: two analysts covered Arteche at the June and July 2026 month-ends and three from the 31-Aug month-end, and FY2028 EBITDA fell from €141.95m (31-May) to €134.0m (30-Jun and 31-Jul) and rose to €142.65m at 31-Aug as the analyst left and returned. So the gap may come from the returning analyst's different EBITDA-to-EPS mix rather than from analysts carrying more of the operating gain away below EBITDA. The costs below EBITDA in the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov), tax rising to 19% in FY2027 and 22% by FY2030 and net financial items near €9m, are the memo's own forecasts; the memo says the market assumes tax stays at 16% and financial costs near zero. The memo leaves the amortisation of SEG Electronics (the German maker of medium-voltage protection relays that Arteche bought in June 2026 for €50.6m), €1m to €2m a year from FY2027, out of adjusted EPS.
  • Implied growth and margin: EPS +23.8% from FY2026 to FY2027 and +17.0% to FY2028, on revenue growth of 16.6% and 14.9%; the implied EBITDA margin rises from 17.7% (FY2026) to 18.5% and 18.9%. FY2027 EPS consensus rose 34% in 12 months against 7% for FY2027 revenue: the upgrades were margin-led.

In your 28 to 31-Oct note you wrote: "ISSUE — Why are FY 2028 EPS estimates falling? MAJOR ISSUE. Ditto EBITDA. It's a 2028-specific issue (2027 is clean)". On the latest readings FY2028 is not falling: EPS +1.3% and EBITDA +10.8% over 3 months; the fall is a 6-month reading (EPS (1.3%), EBITDA (1.0%), from the 30-Apr month-end).

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion market note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Arteche passes it over 3 months for all three FYs, fails it over 6 months for FY2028, and has had no upgrade for five and a half weeks.

The estimates Arteche's SP is discounting are cuts, and the sources read show none yet. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (1-Nov) rates cuts of 5% or more to FY2027 and FY2028 consensus EPS at about 60% and 65%, and a 70% chance that the first FY2027 EBITDA range tops out below consensus. FY2026 consensus EBITDA (€103.7m) sits 3.3% above the top of Arteche's raised guidance (€100.4m, 28-Jul; earnings review). Hypothesis: the SP is leading those cuts, as your 3-Nov note expects ("Almost always, price moves first"), and as it led the June 2026 cut (one of two analysts cut FY2027 EPS at the June and July 2026 month-ends, although FY2027 consensus EPS rose 6.3%, from €1.190 to €1.265, between the May and June month-ends because coverage fell from three analysts to two).   • 26-Oct to 3-Nov, €9.95 to €10.42 (+4.7%): the sector's bounce, in line with the median of 14 grid peers (+3.9%), on up days averaging 0.80x the 50D average volume on the volume script's basis (0.75x on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's).
  • Wed 4-Nov, +1.2% to €10.55, intraday high €11.14 (+6.9%): Alfen's own day, its results day, while its industry fell 0.3%, its cohort peers were flat and Siemens Energy fell 8.0%; it closed at 36% of the range on 2.67x volume.
  • Thu 5-Nov, (4.4%) to €10.09: mostly the sector's, with its industry (1.0%), its cohort peers (3.6%) on average, Siemens Energy (4.7%) and ABB (4.2%).
  • Fri 6-Nov, +1.3% to €10.22: against a falling market and a falling cohort, but with the large grid names: its industry fell 0.6%, the Stoxx Europe 600 1.0% and its cohort peers 0.9% on average, while Siemens Energy, the stock that tracks Alfen's daily moves most closely, rose 3.7% on 1.70x volume and Nexans 4.4% on 2.01x. Alfen rose on 0.99x volume, after an intraday low of €9.92. The cause is not in the sources read.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026 was read; it tags Alfen "Encouraging Q3 Profitability Beat Offset by Narrowed Guidance [+]" and finds no other news in the week.

  • Third-quarter results, 4-Nov (under the report's company filings and press releases heading; the report names no underlying document, only a source tag, "ARS", that it does not explain and also puts on its own "no material news" lines): revenue (5%) to €99.1m; gross margin 30.8% from 28.7%, "driven by the release of project contingencies on energy storage"; the credit facility renewed to October 2029 on the same terms.
  • Guidance narrowed, 4-Nov (the same company filings and press releases heading of the report): FY2026 revenue in "the upper half of €435m–€475m" and adjusted EBITDA margin in "the lower half of 4%–7%". Item 4 (Consensus by named FY) sets this against the last note.
  • Deutsche Bank, 4-Nov: adjusted EBITDA of €4m beat consensus of €2.3m, but the narrowed guidance implies fourth-quarter margins "below 2%"; Deutsche Bank stays on the "Sideline" until 2027 margin expansion is clearer.
  • Hypothesis: a partly one-off beat and a lower implied fourth quarter explain why the 4-Nov rally was sold and analysts cut; the sources read do not state the cause of the 4-Nov reversal from €11.14 to a €10.55 close.

The MAs: Alfen is below all seven, and only its 5D and 10D MAs have turned up. It closed 1.1% below its 5D MA (€10.33), 0.4% below its 10D (€10.26), 1.4% below its falling 20D MA (€10.37), 8.7% below its falling 50D (€11.19), 14.5% below its edging-down absolute 200D (€11.96), 17.0% below its falling 100D (€12.32) and 20.1% below its 150D (€12.79), the only one still rising and the MA from which the tranches' stop-loss is set. The 50D has been below the absolute 200D since 14-Oct; after the March low it took eight weeks to cross back.

Healthy retest or broken trend? Alfen against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: fails ((0.42%) over 20 sessions).
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: passes (€12.79 against €11.96).
  • The 50D MA above the 150D MA and the absolute 200D MA: fails (€11.19).
  • The SP above each MA: fails; below all seven.
  • Near its 52W high: fails; 48.0% below.
  • Away from its 52W low: partly; 23.7% above the €8.26 closing low of 20-Mar.
  • Stage 3 (topping) red flags: the largest one-day fall since the advance began, no (27-May, (10.2%)), though 5-Nov's (4.4%) came the day after a profit beat; wider swings, yes (average daily range 4.2% over 10 sessions against 2.7% over the 20 before); lower lows, yes on closes since May, and 6-Nov's €9.92 undercut the €9.95 closing low intraday; more down days than up days, yes (36 down days against 22 up days since 17-Aug); a light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume, no in its strict form; the SP tracking a rising MA, not applicable.
  • Verdict: a broken trend, not a healthy retest. A close below €9.91 on above-average volume would confirm the decline continues; a close above the 50D MA (€11.19, 9.5% above the close) with €9.91 intact would start to repair it.

  • Sector RS: 75 on 2-Oct, 13 on 23-Oct, 25 at the 30-Oct month-end, 4 on 4-Nov (the lowest in the data file, which starts in October 2025) and 5 on 6-Nov: 45 points below the line of 50 that the 30-Nov month-end reads.

The order of the turn: Alfen first and alone, then its sector, then its industry. Alfen's Stock RS fell from 87 on 10-Jul to 30 on 31-Aug while its Sector RS eased from 92 to 73; the sector broke from 2-Oct and the industry from 9-Oct. Alfen broke on its own; the rotation then removed any support.

Precedent: at Alfen's two earlier Stock RS lows its sector was strong and carried the recovery; this time the sector is at its own low. On 31-Oct-2025 Alfen's Stock RS was 3 with Sector RS 86, and the SP rose 18.9% from that day's close (€9.62) to 4-Feb-2026 (€11.44), 19.3% from its 3-Nov-2025 low (€9.59). On 20-Mar-2026 it was 12 with Sector RS 74, and the SP rose 138% to 26-May. Hypothesis: a sector recovery is now a precondition of an Alfen recovery but not enough on its own, because Alfen broke before its sector. Test: Sector RS above 50 on a Friday reading, with Alfen's Stock RS rising with it.

4. Consensus by named FY

Alfen's EBITDA consensus is falling for every year and its revenue consensus is edging up: more revenue at a lower margin, the pattern since the half-year results of 18-Aug. (Years to December; EUR; changes to 6-Nov.)

  • EBITDA: FY2026 €21.65m, (0.4%) over 1 month, (14.4%) over 3 and (12.3%) over 6; FY2027 €27.95m, (2.9%), (9.0%) and (3.2%); FY2028 €33.71m, (2.2%), (6.5%) and (2.2%). The cuts came on 4-Nov, with a small further cut on 6-Nov.
  • Revenue: FY2026 €458.1m, FY2027 €495.5m, FY2028 €537.2m, each up 0.6% over 1 month and 1.3% to 1.8% over 3.
  • EPS: FY2026 a loss of €(0.140), against a €0.18 profit three months ago; FY2027 €0.350, (1.3%) over 1 month, +11.8% over 3; FY2028 €0.460, (7.8%) and (4.6%). On six or seven analysts and a near-zero base, EBITDA is the cleaner line.

The later years are cut as well as the nearer one, so analysts are lowering the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward. Implied EBITDA margins are 4.7% (FY2026), 5.6% (FY2027) and 6.3% (FY2028). Implied EBITDA growth of 29.1% in FY2027 and 20.6% in FY2028, on revenue growth of 8.2% and 8.4% in the same years, places the recovery in the margin, and each round of cuts moves it out.

The guidance question from the last note is settled: Alfen narrowed its FY2026 guidance on 4-Nov, and the narrowing was negative on margin. The previous note (Jefferies, via the 4-Nov report) called it reiterated at €425m to €475m; the company's range (4-Nov, under the company filings and press releases heading of Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026) is €435m to €475m, now narrowed to its upper half, with the margin narrowed to the lower half of 4% to 7%.

  • The arithmetic (the APM's calculation): the guided range is €455m to €475m of revenue at a 4.0% to 5.5% margin, or €18.2m to €26.1m of adjusted EBITDA. Nine months delivered €20.4m (first half €16.4m, third quarter €4.0m) on €360.6m of revenue. Subtracting that €20.4m from the full-year range puts the fourth quarter between €(2.2)m and €5.7m of adjusted EBITDA.
  • Deutsche Bank (4-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026): fourth-quarter margins "drop below 2% at the midpoints" of the narrowed ranges. At the midpoints of the narrowed ranges the implied fourth quarter is €1.7m of adjusted EBITDA on €104.4m of revenue, a 1.6% margin. Consensus already sits below the midpoints, at a fourth quarter of €1.25m on €97.5m, a 1.3% margin.
  • Quality of the beat: the gross-margin gain came from "the release of project contingencies on energy storage" (4-Nov, under the company filings and press releases heading of Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026), so part of the €1.7m adjusted EBITDA beat over consensus (€4m against €2.3m) was a release, not a run-rate gain.

Against the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): its FY2026 EBITDA forecast of €18.3m sits at the floor of the narrowed guidance (€18.2m); consensus sits near the middle. The memo forecast "three downgrade steps", the first an FY2026 EBITDA cut "of 10% or more by February". So far FY2026 EBITDA is down 2.3% since 3-Nov; a 10% cut (to about €19.9m) stays inside the guided range. Its storage-intake, substation and charger-price tests cannot be judged from the sources read.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". Alfen fails it on EBITDA, the line its analysts and its guidance use: FY2027 EBITDA is down 9.0% over 3 months and 3.2% over 6, FY2028 down 6.5% and 2.2%. Only revenue and the noisy FY2027 EPS line are rising.

On the way up Alfen follows its cohort; it does not lead it.

On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "My hunch is to recycle capital into stocks with positive EPS momentum that are oversold for thematic sector reasons, à la NKT, not Alfen". NKT's one-year-forward EPS is up 5% over three months, and on 6-Nov it rose 2.1% on 1.49x volume, closing at its high; it is still 4.7% below its 50D MA with Stock RS 31: oversold, not yet turning.

What the peers imply. A grid-sector rebound would lift Alfen without repairing its own break or changing a rule outcome. Arteche, the other holding in Alfen's cohort, also sells at the Tue 10-Nov session, so the rules take you out of both grid holdings at once.

FY2028 EPS has fallen more slowly than FY2027 over every period, so the trajectory is being pulled later, not raised.

Analysts keep raising Theon's revenue and EBITDA for every year while cutting FY2027 and FY2028 EPS; the SP has followed the EPS line.

The trajectory: EPS is pulled into FY2026 and pushed out to FY2028. Implied growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 2.3% for EPS against 25.1% for revenue and 27.1% for EBITDA; from FY2027 to FY2028 it is 19.6% for EPS against 17.0% and 17.9%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 27.0% (FY2026) to 27.4% and 27.6%. So the operating case holds and the gap opens below EBITDA: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (5-Nov) puts it to interest on debt peaking at about €575m in mid-2027, tax and the Exosens mark, and calls the FY2026 EPS median a weak signal because brokers' bases differ.

Against your rule: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule" (your 3-Nov note, "[PM] Market + FfF; Sector price trends @ 03-Nov-26"). Theon fails it on FY2027 EPS (down 3.9% over 3 months, 2.9% over 6) and passes on revenue and EBITDA. On 28-Oct you wrote: "Exit unless we can prove strong upgrade scope". The memo expects that "The breadth of EPS revisions reads negative in most months" until the balance-sheet question closes, so the proof you asked for is unlikely before the March 2027 results.

The test: the third-quarter trading update, Monday 9-Nov after the close. The memo calls the nine-month book-to-bill at 1.0 or more "the number that matters", expects revenue of about €132m, a margin near 23.5% and working capital near its seasonal 45% peak, and says neither of the last two would be a miss. For the 9-Nov update it puts the chance that guidance is held at 80%, of a tightening to "at least 600" at 15% and of a cut at 5%. Net debt is the line to watch: it printed worse than consensus at both 2026 prints.

What the rules say today, tranche by tranche:

  • The whole holding (tranches 1 and 2, 3.46%): four whole-holding sell signals, two re-read at the 6-Nov Friday reading (Industry RS 0; the relative 200D MA down 1.53% over the month) and two from the 30-Oct month-end (Sector RS with Stock RS; SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS). What the rules say for Avanza now, and what decides the next step

  • Sell-side estimates: cuts dominate the cohort.   • Company guidance and communications: Flutter's third quarter (4-Nov) brought a net loss of $1.15bn, adjusted EBITDA (25%) to $360m and the FY2026 adjusted EBITDA midpoint cut by $160m to about $2.50bn, citing adverse sports results, United Kingdom gaming taxes and Brazil (Cambs Times, 4-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026); it paused its buyback to prioritise deleveraging.   • Brokers' logic on Flutter: Bernstein cut its PT to $95 from $100 on a prolonged investment phase (4-Nov); Deutsche Bank to $119 from $141 on a credit-focused review (MT Newswires, 5-Nov); Morgan Stanley held Overweight and $155 (4-Nov); Redburn held Neutral (4-Nov); all via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026. For Bally's Intralot, a peer paying down debt instead of buying back stock is the opposite of its own debt-funded path (the A&J Memo's red flags), yet brokers have not rewarded it yet: Bernstein cut Flutter's PT to $95 from $100 and Deutsche Bank to $119 from $141 on its credit-focused review.

4. Consensus by named FY

CVS Group's FY2027 is its year to June 2027; FactSet consensus in pence and £m, to 6-Nov-2026.

CVS Group's SP has risen 19.8% in a month on estimates that rose about 1%: the price is leading, and the estimates have not followed.

  • FY2027: EPS 95.6p, +1.1% over 1 month, 0.0% over 3 and 12 months, +0.7% over 6; revenue £751.0m, +0.4% over 1 month; EBITDA £150.8m, +0.3%.
  • FY2028: EPS 104.1p, +0.9% over 1 month, (0.7%) over 3, (0.6%) over 6, (1.4%) over 12; revenue £785.3m, +0.6% over 1 month; EBITDA £159.1m, +0.6%.
  • FY2029: EPS 113.1p, +1.6% over 1 month, (3.4%) over 3, (1.2%) over 6; revenue £823.8m, +2.1% over 1 month; EBITDA £167.5m, +1.1% over 1 month and (3.7%) over 6.
  • Coverage moves: analysts with an FY2027 EPS estimate read 10 on 6-Nov, 11 on 5-Nov and 12 on 3-Nov (the previous runs' files), so part of the change may be coverage, not upgrades.

The trajectory: the later years rise faster over one month but are lower over three and six, so analysts have not raised the trajectory. Implied EPS growth is 8.8% from FY2027 to FY2028 and 8.6% to FY2029; revenue 4.6% and 4.9%; EBITDA 5.5% and 5.3%. EPS grows about 1.6 times as fast as EBITDA, which fits the buyback shrinking the share count. The implied EBITDA margin is 20.1%, 20.3% and 20.3%, against 19.9% in FY2026 and the 19% to 23% target.

Against your rule: your 3-Nov market note says "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". CVS Group still fails it: FY2027 EPS is flat over 3 months, and FY2028 and FY2029 EPS are lower over 3 and 6 months. The same note says "Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"; CVS Group fits it so far.

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov): it expects the three events before April 2027 (late November, January and 4-Mar-2027) to show group like-for-like growth of about 1.5% against 2.7% a year earlier, and FY2027 EPS consensus to drift to "about 92p to 94p by March", 2% to 4% below today's 95.6p. Implication: the memo expects the 25-Nov trading update to read as deceleration after a 20.4% rise in the SP.

  • The average PT was 1,640p on 6-Oct, 1,626p from 12 to 28-Oct, and 1,605p since 3-Nov (data file, daily series), a fall of 2.5% on 3-Nov.
  • Ratings went from 9 Buys and 2 others of 11 at the October month-end to 8 Buys and 3 others of 11 on 6-Nov; no analyst rates CVS Group a Sell. Leaving aside the June 2026 feed gap, 8 Buys is the fewest since the May 2026 month-end (7 of 10).
  • Ratings follow valuation more than momentum: Buys rose after falls (March 2025, July 2026) and slipped at highs (February 2026), as now.

The gap between CVS Group's average PT and its SP is 17.0%, narrower than at any month-end since August 2023 (14.6%); the next narrowest was 21.0% in May 2025. It was 22.5% at 30-Oct and about 44% at the 2-Oct low. News, not the gap, set the direction. Jefferies' 1,400p (Hold) is 2.0% above the 6-Nov close.

Hypothesis: investors are buying veterinary and pet-care exposure as a group, and CVS Group follows. Test: whether CVS Group holds 1,328p if Pets at Home and Vimian pull back from their highs.

Hexatronic's SP fell 20.5% in five sessions with no change in any estimate since 2-Nov: the SP is moving ahead of the estimates.

  • Hexatronic's FY2026 to FY2028 EPS, revenue and EBITDA and its average PT have not moved since 2-Nov (FY2027 EPS SEK 2.946)
  • On 3-Nov you wrote in your market note: "Almost always, price moves first". The fall is the market pricing either a cut not yet in consensus or a de-rating on unchanged earnings. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo records that in 2023 "the multiple fell one to two quarters before earnings did", and judges "a 20-35% drawdown on intact fundamentals" plausible for a stock with "one visible broker and no anchor owner"
  • Your rule in the same note ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule") is met: Hexatronic's FY2027 EPS is up 10.9% over 3 months and 14.6% over 6 months

Analysts lifted the near year faster than the far year after the 22-Oct report: earnings pulled forward, not a higher trajectory.

  • One month (30-Sep to 6-Nov): EPS FY2026 +7.2%, FY2027 +10.3% (SEK 2.672 to 2.946), FY2028 +7.0%; revenue +3.3%, +3.8% and +3.2%; EBITDA +5.1%, +4.5% and +3.5%
  • Implied EPS growth from FY2027 to FY2028 narrowed from 15.0% to 11.5% in the month, because FY2027 rose faster
  • Implied EBITDA margins: FY2026 13.4%, FY2027 14.0%, FY2028 14.3%. FY2027 EPS rose more than twice as fast as FY2027 EBITDA (10.3% against 4.5%): the extra gain comes from below the EBITDA line (lower interest after leverage fell to 1.3 times, earnings review, 22-Oct), not from margin

The revision burst the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo expected has happened and is larger, which leaves consensus above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's own view. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo expected "a +5-9% revision burst", "the Q3 print being recorded, not new information"; FY2027 EPS rose 10.3%. Consensus FY2027 EPS now sits 6.0% above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's SEK 2.78, and the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo puts the chance of a cut at "about 40%" at each report from mid-2027. The upgrade momentum that carried the SP since February has no scheduled support before the 4-Feb-2027 report.

On 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "FX is likely to be a big headwind, similar to Xvivo - major fundamental risk; read the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo". The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's figure: a 10% stronger krona removes about 9% of reported revenue but only about 3% of EPS, "because production is local". The krona is a small earnings risk for Hexatronic.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

No analyst is cutting Hexatronic: four Buys of four and Net Upgrade Breadth of +100% on every measure, so the fall has opened the widest gap to the average PT since June 2025.

  • Ratings: four Buys of four at the October month-end and on 6-Nov, from three Buys and one other at 30-Sep; five analysts carry estimates
  • Net Upgrade Breadth on 6-Nov, five of five raising: EPS, revenue, EBITDA and PT all +100%, from +20% (revenue) and 0% (PT) at the 30-Sep month-end. Revenue and PT breadth agree; nothing diverges
  • Latest PT changes in the sources read: SB1 Markets kept its Buy and raised its PT from SEK 50 to SEK 52 on 29-Oct; Redeye raised its Base Case from SEK 44 to SEK 46 on 23-Oct (Finwire via Börsvärlden, 23 and 29-Oct)

Hexatronic's average PT (SEK 50.2) now sits 37.9% above its SP, from 5.2% at the 29-Oct high and 31.9% on 5-Nov; analysts have not followed the SP down.

  • Precedent, against the holding: the gap last stood above 30% from the September-2024 to the June-2025 month-ends (45.8% to 115.9%) as Hexatronic's SP fell from 51.62 to 24.85 and analysts lagged; it closed when the average PT was cut 40% at the July-2025 month-end and every Buy was withdrawn
  • Precedent, for the holding: in July 2026 the gap reached 25.7%, estimates kept rising, and Hexatronic rose 30.6% from the 29-Jul low to 29-Oct

Hypothesis: Hexatronic's analysts rate on results, not on price, so their silence tells you little before 4-Feb-2027. For it: the last non-Buy upgraded after the third-quarter beat; every Buy went at the July-2025 month-end after the 2024 and 2025 misses. Against it: a Buy was dropped at the March-2026 month-end with no report, and another during June's 31.3% fall. The breadth rests on a very small set: one of the five analysts cutting would move revenue or PT breadth from +100% to +60%, and breadth goes below zero once more analysts cut than raise in the counting window.

What would change this reading: a PT cut or rating change from Redeye or SB1 Markets before the 30-Nov month-end, the reading day for SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero with Stock RS below 50.

9. Peers

Hexatronic's peers pass three of your four peer checks and fail the fourth: their estimates, newsflow and guidance are rising while their SPs fall.

  • Sell-side estimates: Deutsche Bank raised Prysmian's FY2026 to FY2028 EBITDA by 6% to 8% (Deutsche Bank, 2 and 3-Nov); Rothschild & Co Redburn raised Amphenol's 2027 EPS 11% and its PT to $107 (30-Oct); Nokia's 3M EPS revision is +4% (all via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026, and the PMS Per-stock Dashboard)
  • Newsflow: Adtran Holdings' third quarter had optical networking up 11% and hyperscaler revenue up more than 200% (Benzinga, 3-Nov); Prysmian's North American Electrification grew 24% organically on United States data centres (Deutsche Bank, 2-Nov); Amphenol's IT Datacom drove a beat (Bernstein, 30-Oct). Against: SpaceX's direct-to-cell expansion was flagged as a threat to telecom equipment (Aktiespararna, 5-Nov), the day Ericsson fell 4.2%
  • Company guidance: Nokia reiterated its comparable operating profit outlook of EUR 2.1bn to 2.6bn (2-Nov); Corning guided fourth-quarter core sales up about 19% (27-Oct)
  • SP technicals: Nokia is 21.6% below its absolute 200D MA (Stock RS 25, from 94 a month ago), Prysmian 17.1% and Adtran Networks 7.1%
  • For the holding: numbers up and prices down is a de-rating of the optical and fibre group on unchanged numbers, the pattern the Sector Digest (13-Oct) called "AI monetization anxiety", and the one Hexatronic now shows (item 4 (Consensus by named FY), Consensus by named FY)

Within the cohort, Nokia turned first (a month ago, with a 9.8% fall on its 22-Oct report while its estimates rose); Hexatronic turned last and fastest. Hexatronic's Stock RS fell 20 points in a month (97 to 77), Nokia's 69 points (94 to 25). Ericsson (Stock RS 49, from 43) moves the other way.

The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo named the test now running: "A Corning or Prysmian disappointment in the next two weeks that takes the cohort down 10% and Hexatronic with it would say the sentiment channel dominates". There was no disappointment, yet Prysmian is down 12.1% and Nokia 16.5% since 29-Oct, and Hexatronic 23.0%: the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's sentiment channel is dominating without the trigger it expected.

Mycronic, the stock outside the cohort whose daily moves track Hexatronic's most (correlation 0.60), rose 1.2% on 6-Nov while Hexatronic fell 4.3%: the link that carried the 4 and 5-Nov falls broke on Hexatronic's own selling day (items 1 and 2).

Hexatronic is no longer the strongest stock in its weakening sector: secunet Security Networks (82) now ranks above it (77); in August Hexatronic held 94 with Hardware at 17.

What the peers imply for Hexatronic: the group's estimates give no reason to expect Hexatronic's to be cut, so the 30-Nov sell tests turn on sentiment and price, not numbers; in July Hexatronic's decline ended the session after Corning's 20% fall of 28-Jul, so a capitulation day in the group, followed by a Hexatronic heavy-volume up day, is the signal to watch.

It is 2.6% above its relative 200D MA, crossed on 5-Nov for the first time since 22-Oct; that relative 200D MA is still rising (+1.59% over the month), more slowly than a week ago (+1.73%). Relative price is 4.4% below its 13-Oct peak and the SP 2.2% below its 13-Oct close of 225.6p: neither has confirmed a new high.

Peers' volume on the heavy days points to Africa, not to the towers. Cellnex (1.0x and 0.9x), Inwit (0.8x, 0.8x) and Rai Way (0.8x, 1.0x) traded ordinary volume on 4 and 5-Nov; Airtel Africa, one of Helios Towers' main tenants, rose 5.7% and 3.8% on 0.9x and 2.7x its 50D average (chart file to 6-Nov): on the results day Airtel Africa traded heavier than Helios Towers itself.

For Helios Towers, the turn is confirmed by a close above 225.6p (13-Oct) on 1.5x the 50D average or more; the October decline resumes on a heavy-volume down day closing back below the 20D MA (212.95p), the first distribution day since 14-Oct.

3. RS

Helios Towers' Stock RS turned back up on Friday, to 71, while its group weakened: Sector RS fell to 35 and Industry RS to 46. "Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70" is now two Stock RS points from both halves being present (Stock RS 71 must fall to 69), 16 sessions before the 30-Nov reading that counts.

  • Stock RS: 86 on Fri 16-Oct, 76 on Fri 23-Oct, 70 at the 30-Oct month-end, 66 on 4-Nov, 64 on 5-Nov (the previous run's reading; the data file holds no 5-Nov entry), 71 on Fri 6-Nov. It was 80 four weeks ago (9-Oct) and 58 thirteen weeks ago (7-Aug).

This week the order reversed: the stock turned up on its results while Sector RS and Industry RS fell, so the stock is now the one leading.

Hypothesis: Helios Towers' Stock RS moves with its 12-month starting price more than with this week's prices, and that starting price is now falling, a tailwind to 30-Nov.

  • For: on 5-Nov Stock RS fell two points on a day Helios Towers beat the Stoxx Europe 600 by 3.3%; on 6-Nov it rose seven points on a day it beat the market by 1.4%, as the 12-month base moved from the 6-Nov-2025 close (171.79p, after that day's 10.8% results jump) to the 7-Nov-2025 close (167.19p). Precedent: the 31-Aug month-end, when both halves were present, is set out in the closing paragraph.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts raised Helios Towers' estimates on the results and raised the later years most: over the month to 6-Nov FY2028 EBITDA rose 4.0% and FY2028 revenue 3.7%, against 1.9% and 2.0% for FY2027 and 0.8% and 1.0% for FY2026. That is the trajectory being lifted, not earnings pulled forward, and it follows the company's new FY2027 outlook (item 1 (SP and MAs)). Consensus did not change on Friday.

  • FY2026: revenue $957.2m (+1.0% over one month, +1.1% over three, +3.6% over six), EBITDA $533.5m (+0.8%, +0.7%, +2.8%), at the narrowed guidance of about $535m; EPS $0.0999 (+12.0%, +5.6%, +7.8%).
  • FY2027: revenue $1,073m (+2.0%, +2.2%, +7.4%), EBITDA $608.0m (+1.9%, +1.9%, +6.4%), EPS $0.1525 (+10.2%, +4.5%, +10.6%).
  • FY2028: revenue $1,161m (+3.7%, +3.5%, +8.1%), EBITDA $672.3m (+4.0%, +3.5%, +8.0%), EPS $0.1955 (+17.6%, +8.5%, +8.9%).
  • The brokers' own changes, all on 5-Nov (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026): Jefferies raised earnings estimates by 2% to 5%; Deutsche Bank raised FY2026 Adjusted EBITDA by about 1% and FY2027 by about 3%, "implying 14% YoY growth in 2027"; Morgan Stanley projects more than 3,000 new tenancies in 2027, 35% above the previous consensus of 2,200.

Part of the EPS rise is a change in who is counted: FY2026 EPS rose to $0.0951 on 3-Nov when Morgan Stanley re-entered the count with no analyst raising (the previous run's finding); the further 5%, to $0.0999, came with raises.

Implied growth and margin: FY2026 to FY2027, revenue +12.1%, EBITDA +14.0%, EPS +53%; FY2027 to FY2028, revenue +8.2%, EBITDA +10.6%, EPS +28%. The implied EBITDA margin rises from 55.7% in FY2026 to 56.7% in FY2027 and 57.9% in FY2028. Consensus FY2027 EBITDA growth of 14.0% sits inside the company's "double-digit" outlook (5-Nov) and matches Deutsche Bank's. The SP led the estimates by a day or two: it turned on 2 and 4-Nov, and revenue and EBITDA estimates moved on 4 and 5-Nov.

Against the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, consensus has moved the wrong way on FY2026 EPS. The memo (3-Nov; the dashboard's copy is dated 4-Nov) expects "a FY2026 EPS print about a quarter below consensus (March 2027: the model's 0.07 USD against 0.09, because H1 2026 finance costs of 125.8 USDm and a 45.6 percent interim tax rate are already in the books)". Consensus has since risen to $0.0999, so the model's $0.07 is now 30% below it. Test: the FY2026 results (projected 18-Mar-2027), which the memo calls "the point of maximum information and maximum optical risk".

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Helios Towers' Net Upgrade Breadth swung from nothing to strongly positive on the results: on 6-Nov five of seven analysts had raised revenue and EBITDA (+71%), five raised a PT against one cut (+57%), and three raised EPS against one cut (+29%). At the 30-Oct month-end revenue Net Upgrade Breadth read 0% and PT Net Upgrade Breadth (17%) (one cut of six).

  • The precedent: the last swing this large was the May 2026 month-end (revenue and EBITDA +83%), after the 7-May first-quarter beat; the SP rose a further week, to 245.39p on 14-May, then fell 23% to 20-Jul while Net Upgrade Breadth stayed positive. Positive Net Upgrade Breadth at Helios Towers has not protected the SP; negative breadth (revenue, March and April 2026) came before rises.
  • Ratings: seven Buys of seven on 6-Nov, from six of six at 31-Oct: one analyst rejoined the count. Every analyst has rated Helios Towers Buy at every month-end since May 2025, so its ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum. The memo's counter-case: "A stock every analyst rates Overweight has no upgrades left"; the first downgrade would be news.
  • The average PT: 288.2p on 4-Nov, 295.4p on 5-Nov, 297.9p on 6-Nov (+3.4% over the three days), 35.0% above the 220.6p close (41.1% on 3-Nov; about 29% at the 13-Oct high). The average PT rises after SP jumps and does not fall with the SP: a weak signal.

The named PT changes and their stated logic (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026):

  • Jefferies, 315p from 300p, Buy (5-Nov): "upgraded long-term free cash flow forecasts"; record third-quarter additions of 924 against a consensus of 662; $1.60bn of recurring free cash flow from 2026 to 2030, above the company's $1.30bn target; return on invested capital up to 14.2%.
  • Morgan Stanley, 310p from 295p, Overweight (3-Nov): estimates "significantly above consensus for 2027 and 2028 tenancy additions"; on the results, revenue 4.7% and Adjusted EBITDA 0.4% above consensus (5-Nov).
  • Deutsche Bank, 290p from 285p, Buy (5-Nov): third-quarter Adjusted EBITDA $135.2m (a 53.5% margin), 0.5% ahead of its estimate "despite slight energy price impacts"; the lowest PT named, 2.7% below the average.

On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "I note MS upgrading estimates and PTs today into earnings later this week". Confirmed (Morgan Stanley, 3-Nov), and Jefferies and Deutsche Bank followed on the results.

Where analysts are cutting: one EPS cut and one PT cut stand in the 6-Nov counts. Neither broker is named in the sources read; Morgan Stanley, Jefferies and Deutsche Bank all raised, so the cutter is another house. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo names New Street as holding the lowest PT and the likeliest first Hold, but whether New Street cut is not in the sources read. The only stated reason for EPS cuts in the sources is Deutsche Bank's (29-Oct, the previous run's sources): "bottom-line projections face pressure because of non-cash foreign exchange losses and rising financing expenses", the memo's own EPS risk.

Test: Net Upgrade Breadth at the 30-Nov month-end, after the brokers' full post-results notes; a cut on the FY2026 EPS line, which the memo expects, would show there first.

Helios Towers trades with Airtel Africa, not with the European towers in its sector, and on Friday it parted from Airtel Africa too: Airtel Africa fell 4.0% and Helios Towers rose 0.4%.

  • 13 to 30-Oct: Helios Towers (9.6%), Airtel Africa (9.8%); Cellnex +6.2%, Inwit +3.8%, Rai Way (0.7%).

  • Hypothesis: the October fall and most of the rebound were a frontier-Africa factor move, as the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo argues, with the 5-Nov results on top. Friday is the first sign of the results carrying Helios Towers against that factor. Against: the cause of Airtel Africa's 4.0% fall on 6-Nov, on 0.90x its 50D average, is not in the sources read, and one session is not a decoupling.

Your four peer checks, for Helios Towers' peers:

  • Sell-side estimates: the European towers are not upgrading: 3-month revisions are flat to negative for Cellnex (revenue (1%), EPS (175%), revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (8%)), Inwit (EPS (3%), revenue Net Upgrade Breadth +6%) and Rai Way (EPS (2%)). Helios Towers alone is being raised.

Airtel Africa is (10.3%) over the month and 17.5% below its absolute 200D MA: a broken trend that bounced and fell back on Friday.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Add only if Airtel is doing okay fundamentally — check it via KQ RR". Partly answered, and Friday weakens the answer. For: Helios Towers' 5-Nov outlook of more than 3,000 FY2027 additions points to continued customer spending. Against: Airtel Africa's second-half spend implied by its guidance is about $220m a quarter (about $1.1bn less $657m, over two quarters), below the 1-Oct memo's Alarm line of $250m a quarter, and its SP fell 4.0% on Friday, 17.5% below its absolute 200D MA.

On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Telcos weak thematically (ergo Helios)", and that "the satellite disruption story and AI disruption story are a negative structural headwind". The sources read this week point the other way for towers: Deutsche Bank's satellite comment above, the memo's "Satellites: a fading headwind; direct-to-cell carried 0.0003 percent of T-Mobile's traffic and Airtel has signed SpaceX as a complement"; and a record 3,435 additions in nine months (company, 5-Nov). No source read addresses an AI-disruption case for towers.

What the peers imply: Helios Towers' SP answers to its own results and to Airtel Africa, but its Sector RS answers to Cellnex and Inwit, whose rally is not estimate-led. Cellnex's 1.5% fall on 1.23x on Friday took Sector RS to 35; a rates-driven fall in the European towers would keep Sector RS below 50 and push Infrastructure's Industry RS towards 30, whatever Helios Towers does.

  • First: the case broadened on 5-Nov and the brokers confirmed it. 924 additions against a 662 consensus (Jefferies), FY2026 guidance tightened upward, a FY2027 outlook above consensus, and five of seven analysts raising revenue and EBITDA, the later years most.

Nothing sells at the Tue 10-Nov session.
  • The nearest sell tests:
    • Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (30-Nov): Stock RS has moved +1, (9) and +13 points over one, four and thirteen weeks; Sector RS has moved (6), +17 and +33 points over the same periods. Any whole-holding sell signal sells Helios Towers when its replacement is bought, under your one-off in/out link, not at a fixed Tuesday.

The Master Dashboard puts it one stage lower, pulling back or basing ("Basing" plausible, 3 of 4 tests; "Healthy Retest" plausible, 4 of 6, on the 50D MA); I put it higher because three closes held the rising absolute 200D MA and the bounce came on 1.68x to 2.51x volume, and not at the third while the highs are lower and the 50D MA sits below the 150D. Both your add triggers are present; only the limit stops an add.

  • What would change this reading: a close above 225.6p on 1.5x the 50D average or more confirms the turn and makes it the third stage; a heavy-volume close back below 212.95p (the 20D MA) makes 5-Nov a failed results rally.

Richard decides.

  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA, and the 50D MA above both: passes, €93.92 and €98.95
