  • Near its 52W high and away from its low: passes, 1.9% below the €104.7 closing high and 49.2% above the €68.87 low of 19-Mar
  • Stage 3 red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, especially after good news: absent on closes (largest since 30-Jun: (4.4%) on 17-Jul); intraday, the 5-Nov fall of 4.5% from the open came on in-line results
  • Wider swings: partly; the 5-Nov range (4.6%) is the widest since 30-Jul, the 10-session average (2.3%) below the advance's (2.6%)
  • Lower lows: absent; closing lows rise from €93.30 (18-Sep) to €100.15 (26-Oct)
  • More down days than up days: partly; 9 down and 7 up since 14-Oct
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly, and heavier this week; the 13 and 14-Oct breakout came on 1.09x and 1.22x the 50D average volume, and three heavy-volume down days have followed at the high (15-Oct, 5-Nov, 6-Nov), but the SP holds above the €101 breakout level
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; 14 closes 0.3% to 3.0% above the 20D MA, nine intraday breaks

Verdict: a retest of the rising 20D MA within an intact trend, every trend-health test passing, with five Stage 3 red flags partly present and the most serious, heavy-volume selling after a light-volume breakout, heavier than a week ago. What settles it: a close above €104.7 on 1.5x the 50D average volume (a breakout), or a close at or below the 20D MA on above-average volume (a failed retest, and day 1 of tranche 1's test).

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: two mechanical-volume days (31-Aug and 18-Sep) sit inside IMCD's 50D average volume and lower each ratio by about 3%. The ratios below leave them out; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure follows in brackets.

The negative first: the results day and the day after were both heavy-volume distribution days at the top of IMCD's range, and 4 to 6-Nov are the heaviest three sessions of the advance since 30-Jun.

  • 5-Nov, the results day: IMCD (1.1%) on 217,490 shares (€22.5m), 1.70x (1.65x) the 50D average volume; it opened 0.7% higher at €105.2, the day's high, fell to €100.45 and closed at 61% of the day's range
  • 6-Nov: IMCD (0.6%) on 203,067 shares (€20.9m), 1.57x (1.52x); it opened 0.6% lower, rallied to €104.75, was sold to €101.75, 0.6% below the 20D MA, and closed at 33% of the range
  • 15-Oct, the session after the €104.7 closing high: IMCD (1.3%) on 270,399 shares (€28.0m), 2.18x (2.11x), closing at 36% of the range, for a cause not in the sources read
  • 4 to 6-Nov averaged 1.59x the 50D average volume; the next heaviest three sessions of the advance, 14 to 16-Oct (1.54x), also came at the high

The distribution-day count has gone from one to three in a week. By your definition (a down day on 1.25x the 50D average volume or more, over 25 sessions) IMCD has three distribution days (15-Oct, 5-Nov, 6-Nov) against two accumulation days (23-Oct, and 4-Nov, the day you bought tranche 2, +0.2% on 1.49x (1.45x) at the day's high). The PMS Per-stock Dashboard's count was 1 on 9, 23 and 30-Oct and is 3 today. All three distribution days came at the €104.5 to €105.5 band that has capped the SP (item 1 (SP and MAs)). On this section's own basis (ratios without the mechanical-volume days), 22-Oct also qualifies as a distribution day: IMCD (0.05%) on 1.28x (1.24x) the 50D average volume. It is a borderline one that cuts the other way: IMCD fell to €98.04 intraday and closed at the day's high of €100.55, well below the band, and the PMS Per-stock Dashboard does not count it.

The up/down volume ratio has turned against IMCD since its high: 0.69 over the 17 sessions since 14-Oct, 0.85 (0.85) over 20 sessions. Over 5 sessions the ratio is 0.53 on close-to-close changes (the PMS Per-stock Dashboard reads 1.02). The advance since 30-Jun is still IMCD's lightest in 12 months: average up-day volume of 157,170 shares, half the March to May advance's 330,827.

The 5 and 6-Nov tests of the 20D MA (tranche 1's) were the last two of nine since 6-Oct, and the only two on heavy volume. Since 6-Oct IMCD has traded below its rising 20D MA and closed above it nine times; the first seven came on 0.84x to 1.35x the 50D average volume, 5-Nov on 1.70x and 6-Nov on 1.57x. The 50D MA (tranche 2's) has not been touched since 6-Oct.

Hypothesis 1, ranked first: supply at the high, holders using the results liquidity to sell at €104 to €105. For it: three heavy-volume down days at one band; the 6-Nov rally sold from €104.75. Against it: both closes held the 20D MA, and on 6-Nov IMCD fell less than Brenntag (3.0%) and Azelis (1.7%), whose volumes were 1.1x and 0.8x their 50D average volume: IMCD's heavy volume was its own. Hypothesis 2, repositioning the SP absorbed, is weaker: absorption closes in the upper half of the range, and 6-Nov closed at 33%. Test: the next two closes against the 20D MA and their volume.

Precedent: IMCD's declines in the last 12 months ended with 5% to 6.3% up days on 1.2x to 1.7x the 50D average volume, not with selling climaxes.

Relative price has not confirmed the SP's high and has slipped since 3-Nov: 6.3% below its 4-May 52W high, against 4.6% on 3-Nov and 2.6% at the 14-Oct SP high; 8.1% above its rising relative 200D MA.

What would confirm the advance continues: an IMCD close above €104.7 on 1.5x the 50D average volume or more (about 194,000 shares; 200,000 on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's basis), with relative price back above its 3-Nov level. What would confirm a turn: a close at or below the 20D MA on above-average volume (day 1 of tranche 1's test), then a close below the 26-Oct closing low of €100.15.

The negative first: on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's consensus mean, IMCD's results produced almost no EPS upgrade. The 5-Nov rise (FY2026 EPS +0.8%) was reversed on 6-Nov, on a day the number of analysts in the mean fell.

  • EBITDA kept part of its rise: FY2026 +1.1% (to €596.0m) and FY2028 +0.9%, but FY2027 (0.1%) after +0.9% on 5-Nov. Revenue rose 0.15% to 0.3%
  • FY2027 EPS is +0.9% over three months, +5.2% over six and (8.2%) over twelve: your 3-Nov rule ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M") is met over six months, barely over three

Hypothesis 1, ranked first: the 6-Nov fall is a change in who is counted, not a cut. For it: on 6-Nov the EPS count fell from 15 to 14, the PT count from 15 to 13 and the FY2027 EBITDA count from 13 to 12; one "other" rating left (3 to 2); and the Net Upgrade Breadth counts still show five analysts raising FY2027 EPS and one cutting, no new cut. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (3-Nov) warns that, with three brokers on another EPS basis, "a move in it when those brokers update or drop out will look like a revision and will not be one". Against it: the sources read do not name the analyst. Test: whether the count and the mean return in the next week.

Trajectory: FY2027 is the soft year. Implied growth from FY2026 to FY2027 is 7.8% for EPS, 3.9% for EBITDA and 4.6% for revenue (FY2027 to FY2028: 9.1%, 7.0%, 5.2%); the implied EBITDA margin is 11.5%, 11.4% and 11.6%. Analysts see the 2026 price tailwind and a pause in 2027, as the memo's "2027 is the test" argues.

The link to the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's 5-Nov test. The reported +18% and 42.1% (item 1 (SP and MAs)) are the base case, 0.1 points above the lower line; organic gross profit, its other test, is not in the sources read.   • Precedent: after the 29-Jul results PT breadth went from +13% to +47% in a month

Ratings follow the gap to the PT more than the SP's momentum: Buys peaked at 14 in autumn 2025 with the PT 56% to 65% above the SP, fell to 9 by April 2026 with the gap at 12%, and have read 11 since July (11 Buys, 2 others, 1 Sell on 6-Nov).

1. SP and MAs

On Fri 6-Nov D'Ieteren's SP fell 2.0% to €178.6, back below the €180.4 close of 4-Sep that it broke on 4-Nov: the breakout has failed on closes after two sessions, on the test the previous note set (a close back below €180.4 on above-average volume, here 1.18x). It is D'Ieteren's largest one-day fall since its 24-Sep low; about 1.2 points of it are the market's (the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.0%, and D'Ieteren's beta to it is 1.22, so the market explains 1.0% x 1.22).

  • The trend: over the 252 sessions to 6-Nov D'Ieteren is up 22.8%, against +14.2% for the Stoxx Europe 600 and +8.4% for Consumer discretionary; over 3 months +0.3%, level with Consumer discretionary and ahead of the Stoxx Europe 600 ((1.8%)). Over the week +3.3%, the same as Consumer discretionary (+3.2%); once the part explained by the market through its beta is removed, +3.0%, which is 0.8 times its usual weekly move of 3.7%.
  • The range: since the €195.44 close of 10-Feb D'Ieteren's swing highs came lower (€186.06, €184.3, €180.4) while its lows rose (€153.86, €158.9, €160.0, €170.4 on 28-Oct); the 6-Nov close is back inside it.

Developments: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026 found "No material news flow" and "No new sell-side notes" for D'Ieteren; its only filing was the weekly liquidity-contract statement (Press Release, 4-Nov: a net 1,575 shares sold from 28-Oct to 3-Nov, under 3% of one day's volume, no signal). The cause of the 5 and 6-Nov falls is not in the sources read.

The legs since the 24-Sep low (closes; industry = Consumer discretionary, equal-weighted):

  • 24-Sep to 16-Oct, +9.9% (industry +3.5%), and 21 to 28-Oct, (3.1%) (industry (1.0%)): both mostly D'Ieteren's own, the first led by the Belron banks report of 8-Oct (Bloomberg Línea, 8-Oct), the second by 27-Oct, the day FY2027 EPS consensus fell 1.7%.
  • 28-Oct to 4-Nov, +7.3% to €182.8 (industry +4.4%, Stoxx Europe 600 +2.1%): the industry's leg, with about 3 points of D'Ieteren's own.
  • 4 to 6-Nov, (2.3%) to €178.6 (industry (0.4%), Stoxx Europe 600 (1.4%)): the market's and D'Ieteren's own, not the industry's. On 5-Nov D'Ieteren traded to €187.0 and closed at its low; on 6-Nov about (0.8) points of its fall is its own after beta. It reversed with the sector's leaders Inter Cars and Sixt (item 9 (Peers)), on more volume than they traded (item 2 (Volume)).

The €180 to €187 zone has now capped six D'Ieteren rallies this year. Of the earlier 2026 sessions when D'Ieteren first closed 6% or more above its 50D MA, only 9-Jan ran on; after the other five (17-Apr to 4-Aug, all at €179 to €186) it fell 5.2% to 12.6% within 20 sessions. On 4-Nov it closed 7.2% above its 50D MA; two sessions later its SP is 2.3% lower. Hypotheses, ranked: (1) the range's supply met D'Ieteren again, with no estimate support behind the rally (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)): for it, D'Ieteren's 6-Nov volume exceeded every sector peer's but Auto1's, and its Stock RS fell 6 points while Sector RS held; (2) a market pause after a 3.2% industry week: for it, the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.4% over the two days and Industry RS rose to 53. Test: a close below €175.9 on above-average volume favours the first; a close back above €182.8 the second.

The MAs: the SP still closes above all five, which sit within 2.4% of one another in the reverse of a Stage 2 (advancing) order: the 20D (€174.80, +6.2% over 20 sessions), the absolute 200D (€173.23, +0.4% over 20 sessions, (0.1%) over 5), the 100D (€173.23), the 150D (€172.07, +0.9%) and the 50D (€170.71, (0.6%) over 20 sessions, +0.2% over 5). D'Ieteren crossed the 20D on 8-Oct (2.72x) and the other four on 13-Oct (1.15x), lost four of them on 27 and 28-Oct on 0.84x and 0.51x, and regained all by 2-Nov on 0.56x to 0.91x: light-volume dips, ordinary-volume recoveries. At a flat SP the 150D passes above the absolute 200D around 25-Nov and the 50D above both around 27-Nov, as January and February's €185 to €195 closes leave the absolute 200D's window: the order turns towards Stage 2 by arithmetic, around the 30-Nov month-end.

Healthy retest or broken trend? D'Ieteren against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: partly, and rolling over: +0.4% over 20 sessions, (0.1%) over 5.
  • The 150D MA above the absolute 200D MA: fails: 0.7% below; at a flat SP it passes around 25-Nov.
  • The 50D MA above the 150D and absolute 200D MAs: fails: 0.8% and 1.5% below; at a flat SP it passes both around 27-Nov.
  • The SP above each MA: passes: 3.1% above the absolute 200D, 4.6% above the 50D.
  • Near its 52W high: partly: 8.6% below the €195.44 close of 10-Feb (6.7% on 5-Nov).
  • Away from its 52W low: passes: 25.2% above the €142.7 close of 25-Nov-2025.
  • Red flag, the largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: present, mildly: 6-Nov's (2.0%), on 1.18x, with the market explaining more than half.
  • Wider swings: present: daily ranges of 2.1% to 3.8% from 2 to 6-Nov, against 0.9% to 2.0% in the seven sessions before.
  • Lower lows: absent: the 6-Nov low (€176.8) is 3.8% above the 28-Oct close.
  • More down days than up days: absent: 5 and 5 in 10 sessions; 15 and 15 since 24-Sep.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: present: the 4-Nov breakout on 1.14x reversed on 1.49x and 1.18x.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: no longer: five crossings of the absolute 200D from 13-Oct to 2-Nov; now 3.1% above it.
  • Verdict: a failed breakout inside an intact base, not yet a broken trend. The lows rise, the SP is above every MA and the 28-Oct retest held on the lightest volume in 25 sessions; but two MA-order tests fail and the reversed light-volume breakout is one of your Stage 3 red flags. What would settle it: a close below €170.4 (the 28-Oct low, beside the 50D MA) on above-average volume breaks the rising lows and starts the 10-day stop-loss test; a close above €184.3 on 1.5x the 50D average or more restores the breakout.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Basis: the ratios below leave out the 31-Aug and 18-Sep index-review and option-expiry days (50D average 62,660 shares), with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's in brackets (64,177).

D'Ieteren's breakout was sold on the two sessions after it: 5 and 6-Nov were its heaviest sessions since 15-Oct, and both were down days.

  • 4-Nov (the breakout): +3.3% to €182.8, at 0.88 of the day's range, on 68,663 shares (€12.6m), 1.14x (1.11x): ordinary volume for a close above a nine-month line of lower highs.
  • 5-Nov: up to €187.0 intraday, closed at the low, €182.3, (0.3%), on 91,921 shares (€16.8m), 1.49x (1.46x), just short of the 1.5x heavy-volume line on shares (1.59x on value traded), with Consumer discretionary up 0.1%. A distribution day.
  • 6-Nov: opened 1.0% lower, closed €178.6, (2.0%), at 0.47 of the range, on 73,798 shares (€13.2m), 1.18x (1.15x), with Consumer discretionary (0.5%) and the Stoxx Europe 600 (1.0%). Below the 1.25x line, so not a distribution day on your definition.
  • Inference: across 5 and 6-Nov D'Ieteren fell 2.3% on 165,719 shares, 1.4 times the 121,946 of its two up days of the week (2 and 4-Nov), in the zone where its rallies have stalled since April.

The heavy-volume days (1.5x the 50D average or more) of the last 25 sessions are still the four of 6 to 15-Oct, all a month old: 6-Oct (+0.9% on 1.61x), 8-Oct (+2.2% on 2.72x, €22.9m, the Belron banks report), 9-Oct (+2.5% on 1.59x) and 15-Oct (+0.1% on 1.87x, €17.7m, closing at the low against an industry up 1.6%). 5-Nov repeats 15-Oct: a close at the low on the fortnight's heaviest volume against a flat-to-rising industry; after 15-Oct D'Ieteren rose 2.0% to €175.9 the next session, then fell 3.1% from that close to €170.4 by 28-Oct, nine sessions after 15-Oct and 1.2% below its close.

Distribution and accumulation: the volume script counts 3 distribution days (7-Oct, 14-Oct, 5-Nov) and 4 accumulation days (6, 8, 9, 15-Oct) in the 25 sessions to 6-Nov: the accumulation sits at the start of the advance, the newest distribution day at its top. The dashboard counts 1 (5 on 9-Oct, 2 on 23-Oct, 0 on 30-Oct), leaving out 7 and 14-Oct, which fit its own definition (a data question carried from the 3 and 5-Nov notes).

Up against down volume: the dashboard's 20-session ratio (average up-day over average down-day volume) fell from 1.16 on 3-Nov to 0.90; its 5-session ratio reads 0.93, which raw shares for 2 to 6-Nov do not reproduce (0.82; a data question). On either basis D'Ieteren's average down day now trades more than its average up day. Over the advance from 24-Sep the script reads 1.08 (4 heavy up days, none down): between the June to August advance (1.02, given back by 13.2%) and the March to April one (1.61, given back by 14.6%), so not one of D'Ieteren's strong advances.

The trend of volume, and volume at the MAs: 79,519 shares a day from 6 to 16-Oct, 42,825 on the 22 to 30-Oct pullback, 68,822 from 2 to 6-Nov (dashboard volume trend, 20D against 50D, 0.83). On 28-Oct D'Ieteren closed just below its 50D MA and 0.5% below its rising 150D MA on 0.51x, the lightest of the 25 sessions, and regained both on 0.56x and 0.65x. Sellers were absent at the MAs and appeared at the top.

Precedent: after the last stall in this zone (4-Sep, €180.4) D'Ieteren fell 2.2% the next session on only 0.66x, yet 11.3% by 24-Sep; that decline ended with heavy up days eight and ten sessions after the low (6 and 8-Oct). This time the first down days came on more volume.

Relative price confirmed the break of the September and October highs, not the August high, and has turned down. It reached 99.46 on 5-Nov, its highest in 63 sessions (above 98.85 on 4-Sep and 98.11 on 22-Oct, below 101.10 on 5-Aug), and fell to 98.16 on 6-Nov: 16.4% below its 52W high (2-Feb), from 18.5% at 30-Oct, and 1.7% below its relative 200D MA (99.88), below which it has stayed since 6-Aug.

Peers' volume on 6-Nov: D'Ieteren's 1.18x exceeded every sector peer's but Auto1's ((6.7%) on about 1.2x); Sixt, Bilia and ZIGUP fell 0.6% to 1.3% on about 0.6x to 0.75x. The 6-Nov selling was D'Ieteren's own and the market's, not a sector liquidation.

Would confirm the move continues: a close above €184.3 (5-Aug) on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 94,000 shares on this item's basis, 96,300 on the dashboard's). Would confirm a turn down: a close below €170.4 (28-Oct) on above-average volume, at or below the 50D MA.

3. RS

D'Ieteren's Industry RS read 53 at Friday 6-Nov, its highest in the 13 months stored, while its Stock RS fell back to 44: the industry turn is now carrying, and the stock has stopped keeping up with it.

  • Stock RS: 44, from 50 at the 5-Nov reading and 35 at the 30-Oct month-end; 38 a month ago, 47 three months ago, 22 twelve months ago. It is 6 points below the 50 line of the Sell Criterion "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50", 26 below the 70 line.
  • Sector RS (Automotive - retail, aftermarket, leasing and rental): 58, unchanged from 5-Nov, from 29 on 29-Oct (its 12-month low was 27, on 7-Oct); 66 three months ago; 8 points above its 50 line.
  • Industry RS (Consumer discretionary): 53, from 46 on 5-Nov and 26 at every Friday from 9 to 30-Oct; the previous high in the data was 46 (January and 14-Aug). Consumer discretionary rose 3.2% in the week, second of 16 industries (Market chart); Portfolio Fitness for Fighting (6-Nov) records "the market is buying the European consumer" against its Double Underweight.

The order of the turn: the sector first (from 29-Oct), the stock with it, the industry last (from 4-Nov). On 6-Nov the industry rose a step and the sector held while D'Ieteren's Stock RS fell 6 points. Inference: the rotation lifted D'Ieteren with its sector; on the first down day for the market D'Ieteren gave back more than its groups.

Precedent: D'Ieteren's autumn Stock RS rallies faded (56 on 4-Sep to 34 by 30-Sep; 47 on 9-Oct to 31 by 29-Oct). The 5-Nov reading of 50 was the first higher high since September, and 6-Nov's 44 is back below the 4-Nov reading (48). From its two earlier lows Stock RS led the +37.0% and +16.0% advances (14 on 12-Dec-2025 to 61 by 23-Jan-2026; 29 at the 8-Jun low to 56 by 1-Jul).

The previous note's refutation test is half met: Stock RS is below 47, and relative price (98.16) sits 0.05% above the 22-Oct high of 98.11. Would refute the turn: relative price below 98.11 with Stock RS below 47 at the Friday reading of 13-Nov. Would confirm it: Stock RS back above 50 with Sector RS above 50 at that reading.

4. Consensus by named FY

D'Ieteren's consensus has not moved since 27-Oct: the SP rose 11.6% from 24-Sep on estimates cut in every FY over 3 and 6 months, and the first down days of the push (5 and 6-Nov, in a week the SP still rose 3.3%) came with no estimate change either.

  • EPS, change over 1, 3 and 6 months: FY2026 (year to Dec-26) €12.75: (2.4%), (4.8%), (6.3%); FY2027 €15.09: (1.7%), (3.5%), (4.8%); FY2028 €17.38: (1.9%), (3.8%), (3.5%). Over 12 months the cuts are (13.8%), (12.5%) and (7.2%).
  • Revenue and EBITDA: unchanged since 30-Sep, after 3-month cuts of (1.5%) to (2.4%) in revenue and (3.3%) to (5.9%) in EBITDA, which followed the 9-Sep half-year results.
  • The trajectory: analysts cut the nearest year fastest and push the recovery out: implied EPS growth FY2026 to FY2027 is 18.4% (16.7% three months ago), FY2027 to FY2028 15.2% (15.5%); the implied EBITDA margin fell in every year (FY2027 8.3%, from 8.5%).

The consolidated revenue and EBITDA lines measure mainly D'Ieteren Automotive. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 3-Nov says to read D'Ieteren "on the proportionate group-share basis, never on FactSet's consolidated lines": Belron (50.3%) and TVH (40%) are equity-accounted. So the revenue cuts, and the (56%) revenue Net Upgrade Breadth the Sell Criterion "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50" reads, track D'Ieteren Automotive; EPS carries Belron and is cut too.

Your 3-Nov rules. On 3-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule". D'Ieteren fails it in every FY. The same note says "Almost always, price leads estimates (when a sector is low in range)"; D'Ieteren's rise from 24-Sep, as its Sector RS rose from a 12-month low, fits that lead, and the 5 and 6-Nov selling is what a price lead without estimate support looks like when it stalls. Against an upgrade soon: the A&J Memo expects FY2027 consensus to be cut 2% to 4% after a cautious framing at the 10-Mar-2027 results. Test: the 30-Nov month-end breadth for EPS and revenue.

  • For your ruling: the upgrades rule is not in the Position Entry and Exit Rules, so for an Early Innings Probing Bet it neither sells nor blocks an add; whether it applies to D'Ieteren is yours.

On your investor relations call. On 4-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "D'Ieteren is up from 165 to 175 L1M, and up 5% in Nov-26. My concern re. a weak IR call still remains". D'Ieteren is now up 3.3% in November on closes (€172.9 to €178.6). The 15-Oct call's content is still not filed; after it came a close at the low on 1.87x (15-Oct), an average PT step down (€239.5 to €237.3 by 21-Oct), a 1.7% FY2027 EPS cut (27-Oct) and now two down days at the top on 1.49x and 1.18x. Hypothesis: holders sharing your concern sell into strength; no source read says so.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

D'Ieteren's analysts rate it Buy unanimously while every estimate breadth is negative: the ratings and PTs value Belron, the estimates track D'Ieteren Automotive. Nothing changed on 6-Nov.

  • Ratings: 14 Buys of 14, from 6 of 7 twenty-four months ago: coverage has doubled. Every analyst has rated D'Ieteren Buy at every month-end since January 2026, through the 21.3% and 13.2% falls: the ratings follow neither valuation nor momentum.
  • Average PT: €234.7, (2.0%) since 6-Oct (€239.5) and +10.5% over 12 months (€212.4 at 31-Oct-2025). Its gap to the SP widened to 31.4% on 6-Nov from 28.7% on 5-Nov, by the SP falling; in 2026 the gap has run from 12.6% (the 10-Feb high) to 51.4% (the 30-Mar low) with the PT between €220 and €240, so the gap moves with the SP alone. The latest named changes were raises on a higher Belron value: KBC Securities to €250 from €245 (Bolero, 16-Sep) and ING to €263 from €237 (Zone Bourse, 28-Sep).

Net Upgrade Breadth, unchanged since the 30-Oct month-end except PT breadth, which eased from +36% (6 raising, 1 cutting, of 14) to +29% on 2-Nov: FY2027 EPS (21%) (1 raising, 4 cutting, of 14), from +9% at 31-Aug; EBITDA (62%) (0 and 5, of 8); revenue (56%) (0 and 5, of 9); PT +29% (5 and 1, of 14), easing from +46% at 30-Sep. No analyst has raised revenue since the March month-end or EBITDA since May. PT breadth against estimate breadth is the case in one line: the PTs carry a Belron listing value, the estimates D'Ieteren Automotive's decline.

Where analysts are cutting: D'Ieteren Automotive. Deutsche Bank's Kerstens cut profit before tax forecasts by 1% after the 9-Sep results "to reflect the heavier-than-expected drag from Automotive retail operations" (earnings review). Jefferies expects Belgian fleet demand to "taper" from 2027 as full tax deductibility of corporate electric cars ends on 1-Jan-2027 (Jefferies, in the AlphaSense research filed on D'Ieteren). The A&J Memo records the widest dispute on the same business: Bernstein's 1.7% FY2028 Automotive margin against Deutsche Bank's near 4%. The four analysts cutting FY2027 EPS and the five cutting revenue since September are not named in the sources read, and Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026 found "no new sell-side notes" on D'Ieteren this week.

Precedent: the same split ran at D'Ieteren's March and April 2026 month-ends (PT breadth +30% and +18%, revenue breadth (25%) and (60%)) across the 20.9% rally to 17-Apr, most of which D'Ieteren gave back by 8-Jun. Test: PT cuts without Belron news would mean the Belron value is being cut; an EPS raise would be the first estimate support for the SP since August.

9. Peers

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so its peers are its sector's (the page's peer table).

Since 24-Sep D'Ieteren has traded with the parts and rental half of its sector, and over 5 and 6-Nov it reversed with that half's leaders.

  • SP technicals: from 24-Sep to 6-Nov D'Ieteren rose 11.6%, with Inter Cars (+15.8%), Sixt (+9.3%) and ZIGUP (+8.5%), against Inchcape ((18.1%)), Auto1 ((3.9%)) and Ayvens ((3.7%)). Over 5 and 6-Nov D'Ieteren fell 2.3%, Inter Cars about 3.0% (its (4.2%) on 5-Nov after +4.8% on 4-Nov) and Sixt about 2.2%, while Ayvens rose about 2.7% and Bilia was flat. On 6-Nov alone Auto1 fell 6.7% at its low (cause not in the sources read); the rest of the sector moved (1.3%) to +1.3% on below-average volume.
  • Sell-side estimates: D'Ieteren's revenue Net Upgrade Breadth ((56%)) and 3-month EPS revision ((4%)) sit with the broken half (Ayvens (67%), Auto1 (64%), Inchcape 3-month EPS (9%)), against positive revenue breadth at ZIGUP (+33%), Sixt (+25%), Inter Cars (+20%) and Bilia (+14%). Inter Cars' 3-month EPS revision is +10%, Sixt's +2%. The one peer estimate change this week was upward: Jefferies raised Ayvens' FY2026 EPS 6.6% on a better cost-to-income ratio, keeping Hold and a €9.70 PT (Jefferies, 30-Oct, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026). D'Ieteren's SP trades with the leaders and its estimates with the laggards.
  • RS: Sixt's Stock RS rose from 36 to 61 in a month and Inter Cars stays at 97, while Ayvens (18), Auto1 (7) and Inchcape (6) stay at the bottom: D'Ieteren's Sector RS of 58 rests on the rental and parts names.
  • Newsflow, guidance and communications: Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026 carries nothing for Sixt, Inter Cars, Inchcape, Auto1 or Bilia; ZIGUP filed its group accounts at Companies House (2-Nov). The latest guidance change on file remains new-car retail's: Inchcape cut its FY2026 adjusted operating margin guidance to about 5.5% from about 6% on 22-Oct, D'Ieteren Automotive's business.

Who turned first: Sixt's Stock RS in October, then D'Ieteren's from 29-Oct; Inter Cars led the reversal on 5-Nov. Sixt also tracks D'Ieteren's daily moves most closely (average correlation 0.27, weak), and the A&J Memo finds the sector explains only 19% of D'Ieteren's daily variance over six months: peers set its tone on days without company news, not its direction.

What the peers imply: with three sector stocks at Stock RS 18 or below, D'Ieteren's Sector RS depends on Sixt, Inter Cars, ZIGUP and Bilia holding up. Sixt's third-quarter results on Thu 12-Nov are the dated test: a beat that extends the rental turn supports the Sector RS above 50 into the 30-Nov month-end; a miss would show whether 5 and 6-Nov began the sector leaders' profit-taking, and would take D'Ieteren's Sector RS towards the 50 line.

What the rules say for D'Ieteren now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most:

  • The breakout has failed on closes: the 4-Nov close above €180.4 came on 1.14x and reversed on 1.49x and 1.18x to €178.6, the sixth stall in the €180 to €187 zone this year; the lows still rise and the SP is above every MA, so the base is intact.
  • The groups are carrying and the stock is lagging: Industry RS 53 (a 13-month high) and Sector RS 58 stand above their lines while D'Ieteren's Stock RS fell from 50 to 44.
  • Estimates have not followed the price: EPS is cut in every FY over 3 and 6 months and unchanged since 27-Oct, so D'Ieteren still fails your 3-Nov upgrades rule.

Tranche 1 (2.59% now, 2.5% at entry, bought 12-Aug, on the 50D MA): clear. 50D MA €170.71, stop-loss €160.47, close €178.6: a 4.4% fall reaches the MA, 10.2% the stop-loss. Your 2-Nov-2026 ruling cleared the tranche's 3-Sep confirmation, kept at the 30-Oct close; judged afresh from 2-Nov, D'Ieteren has not closed at or below the MA.

The nearest sell tests:

  • The 10-day stop-loss rule is nearest, and moved closer on 5 and 6-Nov: the close stood 4.6% above the 50D MA on 6-Nov, 6.8% on 5-Nov, 1.5% a week earlier (30-Oct), 1.5% below four weeks earlier (9-Oct) and 6.3% above thirteen weeks earlier (7-Aug). A 4.4% fall is 1.2 usual weekly moves; at a flat SP the rising MA (€172.75 by 30-Nov) narrows it to 3.3%.
  • How a test ends depends on its volume: from day 2 the exit is confirmed when the volume after day 1 averages 0.75x the 50D average. D'Ieteren's 17 to 20-Aug test (0.58x to 0.74x) and 28-Oct test (0.51x) ended without a confirmed exit; its 2 to 3-Sep test confirmed on 1.10x. Every session since 2-Nov traded at 0.91x or more: a pullback to the MA on this week's volume would likely confirm on day 2 and sell at the following Tuesday session; one on October's pullback volume would not.
  • About €160 to €160.5, 10% below the close, brings three lines together: the stop-loss (€160.47), relative price 25% below its 52W high (€160.22 at a flat market, read at 30-Nov) and the 24-Sep low (€160.0). The MA test acts long before.
  • The six Sell Criteria, information only: at Fri 6-Nov Industry RS (53) cleared its line of 30 (26 at 30-Oct), and the relative 200D MA read as falling ((0.80%) over the month), a sell signal for a Core Investment. At a flat relative price it deepens to about (1.7%) in ten sessions as February's relative prices (about 112, against 98.2 today) leave the relative 200D MA's window: the relative 200D MA stops falling from day to day only if relative price rises about 16% to 20%, to the 114 to 117 of early February now leaving its window, and its fall over the month ends at Fri 13-Nov only if relative price rises about 53% this week (about 35% by 20-Nov), so it reads as falling at Fri 13-Nov and, at a flat relative price, through December. At the 30-Nov month-end, Stock RS (44) decides the test "SS Sales or SS PT Net Upgrade Breadth below zero AND Stock RS below 50": it needs 50 or more, because revenue Net Upgrade Breadth stands at (56%); Sector RS (58) clears its test if it holds above 50.

The record: under today's rule tranche 1's first exit would have been confirmed on 3-Sep (volume after day 1 at 1.10x), selling at the Tue 8-Sep session at about €174.1. D'Ieteren then fell 8.1% to €160.0 (24-Sep) and now, at €178.6, stands 2.6% above the €174.1 sale price: the rule would have avoided the fall and missed the recovery, at about no cost.

What decides the next step:

  • The rules allow an add: tranche 1 is clear; one tranche, 3.41% of room below the 6% limit on current value; the next tranche would be 3.0% (0.5% of capital lost at a stop-loss 10.2% below the close, capped at 3%). Consumer discretionary holdings total 4.46% of capital (D'Ieteren and Ballys Intralot), far inside the 30% cap.
  • Where D'Ieteren stands for an add: the first stage, pulling back or basing (basing), on this note's judgement; the Master Dashboard reads none of the three (Pulling Back within an uptrend 2 of 4, Basing 2 of 4, Healthy Retest 3 of 6 on the 50D MA with the SP 4.62% above it, a volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4). The previous note's second-stage call named a close back below €180.4 on above-average volume as the line back to basing, and 6-Nov met it.
  • Add triggers: a positive retest of a rising MA absent; a broadening of the case partly present (the groups' RS only). Your 3-Nov note's "More dynamic up-sizing of probing bets when they break out" finds no breakout here at the 6-Nov close.
  • Dates: Sixt's third-quarter results, Thu 12-Nov; the next reading day, Fri 13-Nov (Industry RS; the relative 200D MA's slope); the 30-Nov month-end; Eric Machiels chief executive from December 2026; FY2026 results, 10-Mar-2027 (confirmed); Belron listing news at any time.
  • What would change this reading: a close above €184.3 on 1.5x the 50D average or more (about 96,300 shares, dashboard basis) makes it the third stage, breaking out; a light-volume retest of the 50D MA (€170.71) that holds and bounces makes the retest trigger present; a close at or below €170.71 starts the 10-day test and blocks an add while it runs.

Richard decides.

1. SP and MAs

Hilton Food broke out of its five-week base on 4-Nov and has held above it for three sessions, but this week it moved with Consumer staples, not on its own news.

  • The trend: Hilton Food is up 19.5% over 3 months and 14.6% over 12; from its 29-Jan closing low of 455.7p it has risen 61.3%. Over the month to 6-Nov, though, it rose 2.8% against 8.7% for Consumer staples, the first month of this advance in which it clearly lagged its industry.
  • The leg and its base: From 11-Sep to 5-Nov the SP rose 11.6%, from 666.4p to a closing high of 744.0p. Inside that leg it based from 28-Sep to 3-Nov, closing between 694.9p and 734.0p (a 5.6% range). The base's top was the 2-Nov close of 734.0p, and that first push to it failed: the SP fell 1.5% to 723.0p on 3-Nov. The 4-Nov close (743.0p, +2.8%) was the first above the base; on 6-Nov the SP moved (1.2%) to 735.0p, only 0.1% above the base's top.

  • The week: Hilton Food +2.2%, Consumer staples +2.3%, the market +0.2%; the dashboard's beta model attributes 2.1 points of Hilton Food's move to the stock itself, but measured against Consumer staples Hilton Food did nothing different.

On 2-Nov (edited 4-Nov) you wrote in your Notion note: "Hilton SP is grinding slightly higher… Consolidating ATM. Up a lot. Hard to want to chase it". 3. RS

All three of Hilton Food's RS readings sit far above the Sell Criteria's lines; the sector fell back on Friday while the industry held its high.

  • Hilton Food's Stock RS: 89 at the 6-Nov Friday reading, from 87 at the 30-Oct month-end and 90 a month ago; 81 or more every Friday since 4-Sep, against lines of 70 and 50. Flat for a month: holding, not rising.
  • Food production's Sector RS: 57, from 68 on 5-Nov (the previous run's reading) and 55 at 30-Oct; 7 points above the line of 50.
  • Consumer staples' Industry RS: 73, from 66 at 30-Oct and 46 on 9-Oct, equal to the 13-month high of 23-Oct; 43 points above the line of 30.

The order of the turn: Hilton Food first and alone, then the industry, with the sector lagging. Hilton Food's Stock RS rose from 19 (24-Jul) to 84 (4-Sep) while Sector RS stayed between 29 and 43; Industry RS rose from 33 (2-Oct) to 73 (23-Oct). The Industry Digest's 30-Oct test (Industry RS above 50 at the 30-Oct and 6-Nov readings supports a rotation over de-grossing) has passed, at 66 and 73. The rotation is lifting Hilton Food's laggard peers more than Hilton Food (item 9, Peers).

Precedent: Food production's Sector RS had three earlier spells above 50 this year: six Fridays (6-Mar to 10-Apr), five (24-Apr to 22-May) and two (10 and 17-Jul). The current spell is four Fridays (16-Oct to 6-Nov). Test, Fri 13-Nov: a fifth Friday above 50; the 11-point fall in one session shows how fast it swings.

4. Consensus by named FY

Hilton Food's EPS upgrades have stalled since 8-Oct, and the later FYs rise more slowly than the nearer one. (Financial year to December; sterling.)

  • EPS: FY2026 52.2p, +1.5% over 1 month, +6.9% over 3, +9.7% over 6; FY2027 56.9p, +1.0%, +6.1%, +8.1%; FY2028 62.1p, (0.7%), +2.9%, +3.9%. No FY has moved more than 0.1% since 8-Oct.
  • The shape: FY2026 has risen fastest and FY2028 slowest over every period, so analysts are lifting the near year (the 3-Sep guidance raise, which Shore Capital called technical: Dalco's exclusion and currency, per the earnings review) rather than the trajectory (implied EPS growth 9.0% into FY2027, 9.1% into FY2028).
  • Revenue and EBITDA: FY2027 revenue £4,798m (+1.3% over 1 month, +5.4% over 3), mostly raw-material prices passed through at cost; FY2026 to FY2028 EBITDA £149.8m, £160.5m and £168.2m (+4.3%, +3.4%, +2.5% over 1 month). Implied EBITDA margins are 3.25%, 3.35% and 3.39%, FY2027's unchanged from three months ago: profit follows revenue.

The month's EBITDA rise looks like one analyst changing basis, not an upgrade (hypothesis). On 4-Nov consensus EBITDA rose by the same £4.2m in each of FY2026 to FY2028 while EPS and revenue did not move; a rise of £4.2m in the mean of five analysts means about £21m (5 x £4.2m) from one analyst, close to the effect of lease accounting (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's FY2026 EBITDA after IFRS 16 is £167.6m). Against it: no source read names a change that day. Excluding 4-Nov, FY2027 consensus EBITDA rose 0.7% over the month.

Hilton Food's SP has run ahead of its estimates: +39.3% over 6 months against FY2027 EPS +8.1% over the same 6 months, so the multiple did most of the work. Your 3-Nov market note's rule ("We cannot own stocks that are not upgrading over N3-6M - its an inviolate rule") is met on 3 and 6 months; the 1-month figure (flat since 8-Oct) shows the momentum fading.

On 28 to 31-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "IMHO the case needs another fundamental leg of delivery for SP to power onwards or it'll retrace hard". The estimates agree that no new leg has come since 8-Oct. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (4-Nov) dates the next legs (NADEC in Saudi Arabia, fourth quarter of 2026; the Polish capacity decision, end-2026; Walmart Canada, January 2027) and expects a 3% to 6% cut to FY2027 consensus after the 7-Apr-2027 results; Deutsche Bank's FY2027 revenue (£4,676m, 5-Nov) is already 2.5% below consensus.

You also asked then: "Q — Risk from Tesco and CEE?" Tesco is the nearer risk; Central Europe is the growth leg. Tesco's move to Scottish beef held West core meat volume growth to 0.6% in the first half (earnings review), and the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo puts another stream of that kind at about 2% of West volume and 3% to 6% of EPS. Polish fresh prepared food volumes rose 26% in the first half, with up to £30m of Polish capacity planned for 2026 and 2027 (Sector Digest, 15-Oct).

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings and PTs follow its SP: the analysts upgraded after the rise, not after the falls.

  • Buys stood at 4 or 5 at most 2025 month-ends while the decline from 905.1p (20-May-2025) ran, and fell to 3 only after the 11-Nov-2025 warning, when the average PT was cut 32% in a month (1,071p to 730p). Buys then fell to 2 at the July and August 2026 month-ends as the SP rose, and rose to 4 after the 3-Sep results. Today all 4 analysts with a rating say Buy.
  • The gap between the average PT and the SP has closed with the advance: 44.6% at the January 2026 month-end, 29.5% at June, 10.9% at August, 3.9% at September (747p against 718.9p), 10.5% at October, and 8.2% now (795p against 735.0p).
  • The latest PT: Deutsche Bank reiterated 850p and Buy (Deutsche Bank Research, 5-Nov, via Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026); the average has been 795p since 8-Oct, a step the 28-Oct note judged a change in the set of analysts, not a raise.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure, but one analyst is cutting FY2027 EPS and no new raise has come since early October.

  • EPS (FY2027): Net Upgrade Breadth +40% (3 raising, 1 cutting, of 5), from +60% (3 raising, 0 cutting) at the July and August month-ends; the first cut since May appeared at the September month-end. EBITDA +60% (3 raising, 0 cutting); revenue +40% (2 raising, 0 cutting, of 5); PT +50% (2 raising, 0 cutting, of 4).
  • Where analysts are cutting: the sources read do not name the analyst cutting FY2027 EPS or give its logic. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo names the visible brokers as Deutsche Bank, Peel Hunt and Shore Capital and puts Peel Hunt's 720p PT "at the price"; its own case for a flat FY2027 (the finance charge, Canada's first-half loss, the April 2027 wage reset) is the memo's logic, not a broker's.
  • The drift: revenue and PT breadth fell on 22-Oct (from +60% and +75%) as raises left the 100-day count, not on cuts; the raises after the 3-Sep results leave it in December, so without new raises revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth both drift towards zero.
  • Where they diverge: EBITDA Net Upgrade Breadth is strongest and EPS weakest, the same split as item 4 (Consensus): the doubt sits below EBITDA.

What the peers imply. Hypothesis 1 (ranked first): a rotation into the whole staples group that lifts the laggards most, with Hilton Food carried along; for it, Industry RS at 73 and Hilton Food's moves matching the industry's this week. Hypothesis 2: money leaving the leader for the laggards; for it, Hilton Food's flat Stock RS and its lag over the month; against it, no distribution day since 15-Oct. Test, by Fri 13-Nov: Hilton Food falling on days Cranswick and LDC rise favours the second. For the sell tests, the peers carry Food production's Sector RS, the reading nearest its line for Hilton Food.

What the rules say for Hilton Food now, and what decides the next step

What matters most for your decision

  • The breakout is real but unconfirmed, and it was the industry's day: Hilton Food closed above its base on 4-Nov on 1.31x the 50D average as Consumer staples rose 2.5%, and dipped on 6-Nov on the week's lightest volume as staples fell.
  • The fuel is the industry rotation, not new estimates: Consumer staples' Industry RS is 73, while Hilton Food's EPS consensus has not moved since 8-Oct and one analyst is cutting FY2027 EPS; over the month Hilton Food lagged its industry and every cohort peer.
  • Your note and the data file disagree on what Hilton Food is: on 2-Nov (edited 4-Nov) you wrote "I moved it from PB to Core Investment"; the data file of 6-Nov still records an Early Innings Probing Bet, and moved both tranches from the 50D MA (the 3-Nov run's reading) to the 100D on 4-Nov.

Each tranche today, under the 10-day stop-loss rule

  • Tranches 1 (1.89% now, bought 1-Jul) and 2 (2.89% now, bought 6-Jul): hold, both clear, with no test running: the 735.0p close is 15.9% above the 100D MA (634.2p) and 23.3% above the stop-loss (596.1p).

The nearest sell tests

  • The stop-loss, the only test that sells Hilton Food as a Probing Bet: on the 100D MA basis throughout, the fall needed to reach the stop-loss was 18.5% thirteen weeks ago, 21.7% four weeks ago, 18.6% a week ago and 18.9% now; on the 50D MA line the data file applied until 3-Nov, it was 17.5%, 13.1% and 9.7% (about four times the usual weekly move of 4.8%); at a flat SP the rising 100D MA cuts it to 14.5% by 30-Nov. Over the week from the Fri 30-Oct close (DKK 971.0) Carlsberg is up 5.7% against 0.2% for the Stoxx Europe 600: with Carlsberg's beta at 0.87, the index's 0.2% rise explains little of that, and 5.5 points are Carlsberg's own (Beverages' rise included), 1.6 times its usual weekly move of 3.3%. It is up 17.4% over 20 sessions (2.3 times the usual), 11.0% over 3 months and 39.3% over 12; the 22.1% advance from the 18-Sep low (DKK 840.6) is the largest since the 34.0% run to 12-Feb.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

Carlsberg's first down day after its 52W high was absorbed, not distributed: on 6-Nov it opened 2.1% lower, at DKK 1,010.0, and closed at the day's high of DKK 1,026.0, down 0.5%, on 1.34x its 50D average volume, while the Stoxx Europe 600 fell 1.0% and Consumer staples 1.1%. 316,437 shares traded, DKK 324.7m (1.52x on value traded, whose 50D average was built at lower prices). The Assistant Portfolio Manager's count calls it a distribution day (a lower close on 1.25x or more of the 50D average volume), but a close at 1.00 of the range after a gap down is buyers taking all the stock offered.

The contrast with February's top is the most useful reading of the week. On 13-Feb, the session after the 12-Feb high, Carlsberg fell 1.8% on 1.67x its 50D average volume and closed at 0.26 of its range, and the 20.0% fall to 24-Mar followed. After the 30-Jul high volume dried up (0.50x to 0.66x for five sessions) and the 12.9% fall began quietly. 6-Nov matches neither: heavier than average volume, but the close at the top of the range. Hypothesis: the selling that met the market's fall on 6-Nov was met by demand. Refuted if the next down day on above-average volume closes in the bottom third of its range, as on 13-Feb.

Carlsberg's heavy-volume days in the last 25 sessions (1.5x the 50D average or more on shares; in brackets, the same ratio with the mechanical-volume days of 31-Aug and 18-Sep (index-review and option-expiry sessions, whose volume comes from index and derivative flows, not from investors' views) left out of the 50D average): 15-Oct, +3.6% on 1.48x (1.54x), 321,386 shares, DKK 299.9m, closing at 0.89 of the range, a brewers' day; 27-Oct, (1.4%) on 2.07x (2.14x), 466,730 shares, DKK 445.1m, closing at 0.11, mostly Carlsberg's own before the statement; 30-Oct, +3.9% on 2.43x (2.51x), 567,548 shares, DKK 551.1m, a 2.2% gap up closing at 0.59, the post-statement reversal. None since.

Distribution against accumulation, 25 sessions: 3 distribution days (27 and 29-Oct around the statement, and 6-Nov) against 6 accumulation days (7, 14, 15, 28 and 30-Oct, 2-Nov) on the Assistant Portfolio Manager's count; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard counts 2 distribution days (0 at the 23-Oct sample). The selling clusters around the statement; the buying runs through the advance.

Up-day against down-day volume (average up-day over average down-day volume): 1.10 over 20 sessions, but 0.91 over the dashboard's 5-session window, which leaves out 2-Nov's breakout and holds 6-Nov's 1.34x down day. Over the whole advance from 18-Sep (+22.1% to 6-Nov, 35 sessions) the ratio is 1.03, down from 1.06 at 5-Nov. That remains the weak point: the advances to February and May 2026 ran at 1.26 and 1.16; only June to July 2026 (1.02), which gave way to the 12.9% fall, was thinner. 5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Carlsberg's SP has caught up with its average PT: the average PT is DKK 1,070, unchanged since 4-Nov after a 2.5% rise in a week, and sits 4.3% above the 6-Nov close (3.7% on 5-Nov), the narrowest gap against any month-end reading in 24 months. Hypothesis: the average PT acts as a ceiling. For it: after the narrowest earlier month-end gap, 5.8% (May 2025), Carlsberg fell up to 19.9% within 63 sessions; after gaps of 10.4% (February 2026) and 10.7% (July 2026) it fell 17.5% and 12.1%. Against it: PTs are now rising with the SP, and the narrow gaps of February to April 2025 (10.1% to 12.1%) were followed by falls of only 7.7% to 8.6% within 63 sessions, with the SP going on to its May 2025 high. Test: further PT raises if the SP holds above DKK 1,070.

The latest PT changes (earnings review; Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 4-Nov-2026): raised by Deutsche Bank (Buy, DKK 1,160 to 1,200, 29-Oct) and Bernstein (Outperform, DKK 1,535 to 1,550, 30-Oct); held by Morgan Stanley (Overweight, DKK 1,075, 4.8% above the close), Rothschild & Co Redburn (Buy, DKK 1,100) and Jefferies (Hold, DKK 950). Ratings: 20 Buys, 8 Holds, no Sells, unchanged since the 31-Oct month-end (21 Buys, 5 Holds and 1 Sell at 31-Aug). Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure, unchanged since the 5-Nov count: EPS +19% (12 raising, 7 cutting, of 26), EBITDA +59% (14 and 1, of 22), revenue +60% (16 and 1, of 25), PT +32% (12 and 4, of 25), against (32%), (29%), (17%) and (22%) at 30-Sep. Where analysts are cutting: Rothschild & Co Redburn cut its FY2027 organic revenue growth by 50 basis points to 3.0% (29-Oct) on "persistent consumer weakness in China that is likely to continue through the first half of 2027"; Bernstein trimmed FY2026 EPS 0.1% (30-Oct); Jefferies has kept Hold at DKK 950 since its 13-Sep downgrade on China. The group, like Carlsberg, is rising on price with flat estimates.

Newsflow and guidance (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026, and the sources named):

  • Carlsberg's own section is tagged negative: its one item is the Morgan Stanley NielsenIQ read of 30-Oct, set out under Developments in item 1 (SP and MAs); the part not given there is that price and mix are "struggling to fully offset volume declines in Great Britain and France". Western Europe beer volume already fell 4.2% organically in the third quarter (earnings review): a known trend, which the SP ignored. The report has no Heineken, AB InBev or Royal Unibrew entries.
  • Diageo beat in its first-quarter trading statement on 5-Nov (North American organic sales (3.6%) against (6.7%) expected; Deutsche Bank, 5-Nov); Jefferies reiterated Buy at 2,200p (5-Nov). The spirits destocking story the Sector Digest calls "a worry clearing" is passing its first test.
  • Coca-Cola HBC raised FY2026 organic revenue guidance to slightly above 7% (Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, 4-Nov), fell 17.9% on 5-Nov on a Russian request to place its subsidiaries under state management (Dnews, 6-Nov), and rebounded 3.4% on 2.86x on 6-Nov; Carlsberg's Russian loss is already taken (2023), so the read-across is to Beverages' Sector RS only.

On 28-Oct you wrote in your Notion note: "Investigate China weakness ASAP - what is going on, and what are the implications for the Carlsberg investment case?" and "Isn't this what Jefferies have been saying? China weakness for Carlsberg, down to sell/hold?". Yes, Jefferies' view is the one that played out. Jefferies downgraded to Hold at DKK 950 on 13-Sep on China, and on 29-Oct put China at about 30% of group volume, Carlsberg's deepest single-country dependency (earnings review). Third-quarter China volume fell nearly 10% on rain, weak sentiment and destocking, against (17%) at AB InBev and double-digit growth at Heineken. Wholesaler inventory was back to prior-year levels by end-September (Morgan Stanley, 29-Oct), so destocking should not repeat; Rothschild & Co Redburn expects the consumer weakness to last through the first half of 2027. The implication: China caps the estimate upgrades your 3-Nov rule needs until mid-2027 unless soft drinks and synergies outrun it.

What the peers imply for Carlsberg. The group sets Beverages' Sector RS, so the Sector RS half of Carlsberg's nearest sell test needs a group-wide fall, as at the 31-Aug month-end. What would tell: heavy-volume down days across the brewers closing near their lows would be the group's first sign of distribution; Friday's gap-and-recover was the opposite.

  • The nearest sell test, Sector RS below 50 with Stock RS below 70 (read at the Mon 30-Nov month-end): Carlsberg's Stock RS is 80, 10 points above the line (+10 over 1 week, +26 over 4, +18 over 13); Beverages' Sector RS is 80, 30 above its line (+4, +26, +22). Consensus still has EBITDA growing 28.3% in FY2027 and 21.5% in FY2028 on revenue growth of 8.1% and 7.3%, so the implied EBITDA margin climbs from 12.4% (FY2026) to 14.7% (FY2027) and 16.7% (FY2028); EPS grows 36.7% in FY2027 and 26.9% in FY2028. FY2028 EPS is up 1.7% over 6 months while FY2028 revenue fell 1.8%; no source read attributes the gap to currency.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Fevertree's SP is pinned under a flat average PT (947.3p): intraday highs of 947.0p (23-Oct), 949.0p (26-Oct), 945.0p (2-Nov), 944.5p (4-Nov) and 955.0p (5-Nov), and no close above 943.5p. The average PT has stood at 947.3p since at least 6-Oct, the first daily reading in the data file (967.3p at the 31-Jul month-end); the median PT, about 875p, is 5.1% below the SP (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 3-Nov). The two latest times the SP reached the average PT (July 2025 and February 2026 month-ends), falls of 14.9% and 20.0% followed within a month. Hypothesis: the flat average PT is acting as a ceiling. Test: a close above 947.3p with PTs rising would break the pattern.

Ratings, PT and Net Upgrade Breadth are unchanged since the 30-Oct month-end, and the sell-side has said nothing new on Fevertree this week (Richard's weekly AlphaSense report of 6-Nov-2026: "No new sell-side notes were identified during this weekly window"). Fevertree has 7 Buys, 5 other and 3 Sells (15 analysts), unchanged since the 30-Sep month-end. Its Buys have followed falls (to 5 after the February-to-May fall, to 7 after the 10-Sep low): ratings that follow valuation, so new Buys are unlikely near the average PT.

  • EPS (FY2027): 0% (6 raising, 6 cutting, of 15), from +50% at the July month-end.

The grading

  • Probable: a sell test gives a sell signal at the reading the rule uses, or the next reading will unless the SP recovers by a named amount.
  • Plausible: the SP is within about one average weekly move of a Probing Bet's MA or stop-loss, or of a Sell Criterion, or one part of a two-part Sell Criterion gives a sell signal.
  • Possible: a reading is closer to its line than at both the previous month-end and three month-ends back (for a Probing Bet's MA, when the distance above it has narrowed over both the last 5 and the last 20 sessions). Add candidates are graded the same way against the add conditions.

The data faults

  • Helios Towers and Avanza tranches dated "0002-11-04" (pms-data/dashboard/data/latest/HTWS-GB.json, AZA-SE.json, tranche 1): the broken date makes the 30% test read price history from 2023 and print "SELL THIS TRANCHE on Tuesday 2026-10-06", which feeds the false "SELL TRANCHE(S) on Tuesday" order states and Helios Towers' add-rule note "a Sell Criterion has fired". Effect: reported as not sales.
  • Alfen's exit dated 25-Aug with no keep record (ALFEN-NL.json): the 2-Nov keep that cleared the August confirmations is lost. Effect: none on the outcome; reported as confirmed 28-Sep.
  • Hilton Food's MA changed from the 50D to the 100D on 4-Nov with no keep or ruling recorded, while your 2-Nov note calls it a Core Investment. Effect: For your ruling.
  • Sizes that disagree with UBS and your notes: Sdiptech (register 7.43%, UBS 6.04% of AUM on 5-Nov, your note 6.0%); Xvivo (register 3.60% in two tranches with tranche 2 dated 17-Jul, UBS 2.06%, your note 2.0%; the 5-Nov readings showed one tranche at 2.43%); Alfen (your 4-Nov sale of about 0.6% against the register's removal of one 0.33% tranche). Effect: the sizes in the note are the register's; the sale sizes at 10-Nov may differ.
  • The previous note's Volex volume was wrong: the 5-Nov chart file then held about 0.83M shares for Volex (also too low for DiscoverIE and Halma); it now holds 3.50M. The previous note's "light-volume lower low" finding is withdrawn. Smaller revisions: Avanza 5-Nov volume 330,465 to 361,861 shares; Carlsberg 251,973 to 256,255.
  • Dashboard volume readings that do not reproduce from the chart files: the 5-session up/down volume ratio (Sdiptech 1.09 against 1.41; IMCD 1.02 against 0.53; D'Ieteren 0.93 against 0.82; Volex 0.0 against 0.11); the "volume trend (20D against 50D)" (Volex 1.01 against 1.14; Hilton Food's 0.58 is the 10-session ratio); distribution-day counts (Arteche 0 though 5-Nov is one at 1.26x; D'Ieteren 1 against the script's 3). Effect: the sections use the chart-file figures.
  • Key-days table dates estimate steps to 6-Nov that the daily series puts earlier (Sdiptech FY2026 EPS (1.1%) on 5-Nov; Alfen FY2026 EPS 6.2% on 4 and 5-Nov; Helios Towers' 12.0% is the one-month change). Effect: corrected in the sections.
